1.            Introduction

1.1           Project Background

The current project finalizes the Nigeria Mineral Sector Support for Economic Diversification
(MinDiver) Project aimed to boost and solidify the Mining Industry in Nigeria. This last part
intends to merge the different data streams into one key deliverable containing a simple guide
to conducting mining business in the country.



With the aim of diversifying the raw material sector and to reduce the dependency on the oil
industry, the government embarked on the objective of securing financing from the World Bank
to develop the mining industry. The result was a $120m IDA credit for the implementation of
the MinDiver Project. Part of this umbrella project is the current mission under the name
“Engagement of an advisory firm to carry out mining investment facilitation activities on
acquired geological information”. After a competitive bidding process, Beak Consultants GmbH
has been engaged to execute this project.

This D4 is a progress report, building on all previous Deliverables, and is named “Report on
Investment attraction policies, marketing, and investor identification”.



1.2           Project Objective

The main objective of the project is to collect, review and organize all geoscientific data of the
country to develop mineral prospectivity maps that allow the selection of targets and the
preparation of the respective prospectuses as investor-ready documents. These mineral
passports act as tangible material for the engagement of both domestic and foreign investors
and other relevant parties, as part of the Ministry’s strategy in attracting investment to the
country to develop the mining sector and further diversify the economy.

The project governance has been structured after Figure 1, allowing a clear and clean
communication flow between the project’s stakeholders. Critical is the Steering Committee
leading the project with the responsibility of reviewing the project performance and taking
sound decisions The Project is managed by the following team:

2.3           Training (Cooperation Procedures)

The transfer of knowledge to the Ministry and NGSA Staff took place during the days 22-23-
24 of April in the ASM Offices. The topics covered from the initial step of data gathering to the
identification of targets. With this ground information, the Staff is capable of selecting
appropriate prospective areas and conducting either further analysis of preparing the
information for marketing purposes. The list of participants is shown in Annex 3: Attendance
Lists Training (22-23-24.04.2024). The topics covered were the following figure (compressed

3.2           Part-project: Target Identification and Ranking

The commodities for this project will be identified by comparing the needs of the national/
international markets with the currently in Nigeria known/ prospective commodities (see
Section 3.4). This requires working on the existing mineral occurrence database available for
the country and the revision on known sources (e.g., published reports) that confirm the
identified locations1.

With the use of Artificial Intelligence (6.1) on these points to identify relevant patterns, it is
possible to identify favorable areas in Nigeria for a selected group of commodities and the
selection of targets2. Finally, a document with a compendium of key details, both in content
and context, will be shared with interested parties and investors. This whole process is

3.4            Identification of the target commodities and of the project area

3.4.1 Target commodities

3.4.1.1 Global Needs

The identification of target commodities in this project is based on the following considerations:
         Review of existing critical raw material lists, e.g. of the EU and the USA,
         Analysis of the expected worldwide demand for commodities in future oriented
          technologies up to 2040, e.g. the research (Marscheider-Weidemann, et al., 2021),
         Further considerations according to current market developments.


When compared to the world market economies and the current needs, such as for Energy
Transition Metals (ETM), most commodities are marked as critical. For instance, the EU has
made the list as shown in Table 3 and the U.S. has defined Table 4. No included as critical but
with reasonable market value prices and future demand are Gold (Au), tin (Sn), Copper (Cu),
U (U), the latter is relevant as energy fuel for the upcoming years.

3.4.1.2 Main commodities of Nigeria and their regional distribution

According to the current knowledge (this project, (Crowe, Garba, & NGSA, 2023), (Olade M.
A., 2020) and general metallogenic considerations in Nigeria the following commodities are
known or could be expected in the main geological / tectonic units
        Archean and Paleoproterozoic domains  Au, (Mn, Cr, Ni, PGE?) and Cu, Pb, Zn, Sb,
         Fluorite
        Pan-African post-tectonic Granites and Pegmatites  Sn, Ta, Nb, Li, gemstones and
         related hydrothermal-metasomatic formations Au, Cu, Pb, Zn, (U?)
        Jurassic Young Granites  Sn, Nb, W, Mo, Li, (REE?), fluorite, (U?)
        Cretaceous Benue Trough  Pb, Zn, Cu, barite, U
        Anambra Basin  Coal
        East Dahomey Basin  Phosphorite, Bitumen
        Sokoto Basin  Phosphorite, (U if reasonable grades can be identified), (Mn?)


For further consideration in this project, we selected commodities as shown in Table 5.

         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




The following rationales were applied for commodity selection:

        Mn, Ni, Cr are listed as critical raw materials, but currently not known in Nigeria in
         industrial grades and quantities, but important geological pre-conditions exist.

        Au is currently the most common mined metallic commodity in Nigeria, and great
         potential for the future.

        PGE are not known in Nigeria, but might occur in the environment of Cr, Ni mineral
         occurrences in ultramafic rocks.

        Cu is a very important commodity, but potential for large Cu deposits is not clear.

        Ta, Nb, Sn, Li have a known potential in Nigeria.

        The Jurassic alkaline complexes are known for their Sn and Nb potential (possibly Li).
         In this geological environment carbonatites (REE mineralisation) may occur as well.

        The Pan-African pegmatites and possibly post-tectonic granites are promising for Ta,
         Li and Sn.

        U was described in early cretaceous sandstones from the Eastern Benue Trough and
         recorded from some areas from the eastern basement domain.

        Benue Trough, and

        Sokoto Basin.

respective deposit entry, thus solidifying the underlying database of the project.




Figure 18: Example of mineral occurrence (pegmatite) location documented in the literature

Although some points are confirmable with this approach, other entries from the original
database remain unverifiable. To discern between the verified and not verified mineral
occurrence locations, in the database field “work done” the following new entries were
generated:

   -     “advangeo-yes”  locations and commodity verified.

   -     “advangeo-no”  either location or commodity could not be verified.

   -     Missing entry  occurrence not verified.

5.             The resource geology of Nigeria

5.1            Geological review

Nigeria lies in a broad belt of Archean to Paleoproterozoic crust remobilised during the Pan
African orogeny between the Western African Craton to the west and the Congo Craton to the
southeast. Within this old crust, one large scale suture zone between old terranes and several
schist belts representing accreted island-arc complexes is known. Only the easternmost part
of the country sits on newly formed Neoproterozoic (Panafrican) crust (de Wit & Linol, 2015)
(de Wit, Stankiewicz, & Reeves, 2008). Magmatic activity resumed in the Jurassic along a
north-south line extending from the Jos Plateau (Abaa, Kinnaird, Ike, & Turaki, 1984) towards
the analogous but older intrusive ring complexes of the Aϊr Massif in northern Niger (Moreau,
Demaiffe, Bellion, & Boullier, 1994). Beginning in the Barremian, the breakup of Pangea led to
the formation of several rifts in western and northern central Africa. In Nigeria, the Benue
trough and several branching rifts formed by the reactivation of Panafrican fracture zones by
transtensional forces (Benkhelil, Dainelli, Ponsard, Popoff, & Saugy, 1988) (Benkehlil, 1989).
In the Cenozoic large scale shallow epeirogenic basins formed in Saharan Africa of which the
Chad basin (with its Gongola subbasin) and the Lullemmeden basin extend into northern
Nigeria (Schlüter, 2008). In contrast, southern Nigeria is characterized by the very thick
sediment accumulations of the Anambra, Niger delta and Danomey basins that formed over
the dilated transitional crust on the Atlantic margin (Hospers, 1965) (Uko, Emudianughe, &
Tamunobereton-ari, 2013).

Therefore the country can be divided into a number of structural and sedimentary complexes
(Crowe, Garba, & NGSA, 2023):

         Basement Complexes: The Western, Central and Eastern complexes and the
          Transitional Tectonic Zone, largely composed of migmatite-gneiss belts with some
          intercalated schist belts, intruded by numerous Pan African granites

         Cretaceous Rift basins: The Benue Trough with its several arms and the Bida basin

         Late Cretaceous – Cenozoic Basins: The northern epeirogenic basins (Chad basin,
          Gongola sub basin, Iullemmeden basin) and the southern continental margin basins
          (Anambra basin, Niger Delta basin and Dahomey basin)




Status    draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 50
          release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




The metallogenic interpretation of these geological and tectonic complexes is given in chapter
5.3.




Figure 21: Overview of the African Precambrian basement structure. The Nigerian basement
 is composed of remobilized Archean and Proterozoic terranes in the west and center of the
  country and younger Neoproterozoic basement in the east. Modified from (de Wit & Linol,
                              2015), (de Wit, Stankiewicz, & Reeves, 2008).

Figure 22: Simplified geological map of Nigeria showing the main structural and sedimentary
              domains of the country. Modified from (Crowe, Garba, & NGSA, 2023).



5.1.1 Basement Complex

The Nigerian Basement belongs to the Trans-Saharan Belt (Adeoti & Okonkwo, 2017) a
collage of gneiss-migmatite terranes with some intercalated mafic ultramafic schist belts that
was widely overprinted ant intruded by numerous granitic melts (“Older Granites”) during the
Pan African orogeny. Commodities related to the metamorphic lithologies are Au, Fe, Mn, Pb,
Cu, Zn, Ni, and Cr. Finally, the Pan-African cycle, responsible for the Older Granites (syn- and
post tectonic), hosts Pb-Zn-Cu veins and Sn-Ta-Nb-Sn pegmatites.

The protolith age of the basement and its metamorphic history has been contentious due to
the remobilization and overprinting in the Panafric an orogeny. Detrital zircon samples derived
from the Western Basement Complex and sampled from the Dahomey basin sediments give
a good overview of the age distribution in the basement (Figure 23) (Oyebanjo, Ekosse, &
Odiyo, 2021).


Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 52
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




Figure 23: Detrital zircon ages in sediments derived from the Western Basement Complex
show the presence of Liberian (~2700 Ma),Eburnean (~2000-2500 Ma) and Mesoproterozoic
(~1700 Ma) metamorphic/magmatic rocks in addition to the widespread Neoproterozoic
Panafrican suites. Modified from (Oyebanjo, Ekosse, & Odiyo, 2021)



In a review of various radiometric dating methods applied to samples of the Western and
Central Basement complexes (Dada, Crust-forming ages and proterozoic crustal evoluton in
Nigeria: a reappraisal of current interpretations, 1998) conclude that the major crust-forming
event in this region was around the Archean-Paleoproterozoic boundary as opposed to a
slightly younger age (Late Paleoproterozoic/ Mesoproterozoic) for the Eastern Basement
Complex (Ferré, Déléris, Bouchez, Lar, & Peucat, 1996) (Ukwang, Ekwueme, & Kröner, 2012).

Several Rb-Sr whole rock ages confirm the Paleoproterozoic age of some gneiss-migmatite
complexes in the Western Basement Domain: at Okene with an upper limit of 2202 + 31 Ma
and a lower limit of 1683 + 56 Ma (Caen-Vachette, 1987), at Ibadan with 2190 _+ 30 Ma (Grant,
Rex, Burke, & Freeth, 1969) and at Igbeti with 1904 _+ 18 Ma (Rahaman, Emofurieta, & Caen-
Vachette, 1983). At Kankun in the Western basement province a magnetite cumulate at the
base of a folded amphibolite shield is also assigned an Eburnean magmatic age, with
metamorphosis and folding occurring in the Pan African orogeny (Annor & Mücke, 1991).

The schist belts are composed mafic to ultramafic greenschist to amphibolite facies
metamorphic rocks with intercalated peraluminous schists and gneiss (Turner D. C., 1983).
Petrographic and geochemical studies of amphibolites from the Iseyin-Oyan and Ilesha schist


Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 53
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




belts indicate that the likely protolith was island arc tholeite (Bolarinwa & Adeleye, 2015).
However, the findings in the Mokuro mafic-ultramafic complex by (Ige, Okrush, Schüssler,
Schmädicke, & Cook, 1998) indicate an affinity for a “rift-related, intracontinental igneous
activity”. In the local picture, the later explanation seems more favourable, whereas the first
may be consistent on a broader tectonic context, noting that the regional shear zone systems
(e.g., Ifewara and Anka) are not related to suture zones but to intra continental rift systems.
Based on lithological characteristics a similarity with the greenstone belts of the West African
Craton and consequently an Archean age has been proposed (Klemm, Schneider, & Wagner,
1984), but confirmation by radiometric dating is lacking. The few available dates suggest the
schist belts underwent metamorphism during the Kibaran Orogeny (1.1-1.3 Ga) (Turner D. C.,
1983) (Grant, Hickman, Burkholder, & Powell, 1972); however, these dates are based on Rb-
Sr ages from adjacent granit-gneiss samples and, therefore, not representative of the Schist
Belts. . Nonetheless, limited zircon (U-Pb) geochronology close to Abuja in the work by
(Ekwueme & Kalsbeek, 2014) have shed evidence on an early-Neoproterozoic deposition for
these supracrustal sequences with ages related to Pan-African Orogeny. Further investigation
is necessary to further constrain concluding ages.

For the Western and Central Basement Complexes a wide range of Panafrican ages from 650
to 450 Ma is given, with some indication that the age of peak metamorphism (amphibolite to
granulite facies) increases towards the west (Dada, Tubosun, Lancelot, & Lar, 1993).
Pegmatites at Guburunde in the Eastern Basement Complex yield Panafrican ages around
580 Ma, with inherited ages and Hf crustal model ages and Lu-Hf systematics suggesting
contribution of older crustal rocks (up to 1.9±0.1 Ga) to the source melt with minor mantle
contributions. The voluminous late orogenic magmatism in the Eastern basement complex was
likely caused by lithospheric delamination (Bute, Yang, Yang, Kayode, & Lu, 2022). P-T
conditions for the peak metamorphism of the Panafrican orogeny are estimated at 600–650 °C
and 8 kbar for migmatitic schist; and 600–700 °C and 8–12 kbar for amphibolite, corresponding
to upper amphibolite to lower granulite facies (Ephraim, Ekwueme, Moazzen, & Modjarrad,
2008).

Dates for the widespread gold mineralization are scarce, but one case study at the Bakoshi-
Kundila gold deposit near Kano (Central Basement Domain) shows remobilization of gold from




Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 54
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




the country rock by a granitic intrusion dated at 683 Ma and a second stage of dissemination
into the wall rock dated at 606–610 Ma (Elatikpo, Li, Liu, & Zhang, 2022).



5.1.2 Mesozoic Rocks

5.1.2.1 Young Granites

The “Young Granites” are anorogenic intrusions - the result of a mantle plume producing
doming of the lithosphere (Wright, 1985). The plutonic to sub volcanic emplacements are
normally the only remaining feature of these intrusive-volcanic complexes. The complexes
represent isolated and nested calderas that are often eroded down to the intrusive complexes,
although in some cases the ring dykes and minor amounts of volcanic rocks are preserved
(Figure 24). The intrusives can be divided into three main rock types: peraluminous biotite
granites, peralkaline riebeckite-arfvedsonite granites, and metaluminous hornblende-fayalite
bearing granites (Abaa, Kinnaird, Ike, & Turaki, 1984).

While not all of the complexes are dated a distinct age gradient from north to south is
detectable, where the northern complexes have a Lower Jurassic age (Fagam: 191 Ma, Ningi-
Burra: 183 Ma) and the southernmost complexes straddle the Jurassic-Cretaceous boundary
(Afu; 141 Ma, Mada: 147 Ma) (Abaa, Kinnaird, Ike, & Turaki, 1984).

         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074

The area of intusive complexes extends to the north below the Chad basin and extends into
   the Aϊr Basement in northern Niger. Modified from (Crowe, Garba, & NGSA, 2023) and

5.1.2.2 Benue Trough

The Benue Trough is a result of a failed rift (aulacogen) linked to the breaking up of the
Gondwana continent during the Cretaceous. Thinning of the crust generated subsidence along
a graben structure with a width of 130 – 150 km and more than 800 km in length (Ofoegbu,
1985). The main faults are old Pan-African faults zones that were reactivated in the Barremian
– Aptian (Maurin & Benkhelil, 1990). Several branches to the west (Bida basin) and to the east
(Mamfe embayment, Yola arm) dissect the basement. The trough is filled with mostly
siliciclastic sediments ranging in age from the Aptian to the Maastrichtian. Syn-rift magmatism
resulted in the emplacement of numerous basaltic sills and some flows within the sediment
pile. The tectonic regime was characterized by transtensional forces, resulting in an internal


Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 56
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




graben and horst structure with several deep sub-basins (Figure 26). Rapid subsidence of the
mostly marine siliciclastic sediments led to the liberation of metalliferous brines that
precipitated lead and zinc along with barite within and near the reactivated fault zones (Maurin
& Benkhelil, 1990). Two episodes of compressional tectonics deformed the basin: In the
southern part (Abaliki anticlinorium) during the Santonian, and in the northern part at the end
of the Cretaceous (Benkehlil, 1989) (Figure 25).




  Figure 25: Compilation of structural features in the Benue Trough. Modified from (Crowe,
                               Garba, & NGSA, 2023) and (Benkehlil, 1989)

  swell structure caused by the transtensional forces reactivating old (Panafrican) tectonic
  zones. The mineralized anticlinal structures are strongly related to basement highs. Data

5.1.3 Cenozoic Sediments

In the late Cretaceous and throughout the Cenozoic many intracontinental areas of Africa are
characterized by large-scale epeirogenic subsidence leading to the formation of large but
shallow sedimentary basins. In northern central Africa the largest such basins are the Chad
and the Iullemmeden basin, both of which extend into northern Nigeria (Schlüter, 2008). The


Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 58
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




older sediments in both basins are of lacustrine, fluvial-lacustrine and deltaic origin, whereas
the younger to recent sediments are predominantly aeolian (Adelana, et al., 2008).

The basement areas in central and southern Nigeria are characterized by uplift and
denudation. Neogene to Quaternary basaltic flows erupted particularly on the Jos plateau and
the Eastern Basement Complex at the southern margin of the Chad basin. The surface of the
flows is often altered to laterite (Adelana, et al., 2008), other laterite occurrences are known as
isolated plateaus and dissected remnants of old land surfaces, particularly in Kogi and Benue
State (Figure 27).




 Figure 27: Laterite plateaus in Benue state, to the southwest of Makurdi. In the radiometric
   ternary colour composite they appear blue-green, indication enrichment of thorium and
                                     uranium and depletion of potassium.




Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 59
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




Following the opening of the Atlantic Ocean thick sedimentary sequences accumulated at the
continental margin over the dilated and subsiding transitional crust. Since the Eocene the Niger
delta has progradated 300 – 400 km into the Atlantic Ocean and deposited sediment into an
up to 10 km deep basin. The crustal subsidence has led to tectonic deformation of the young
sediments with extension and block rotations on listric faults in the basin centre and thrust
structures at the delta front (Figure 28). The complex tectonics resulted in the formation of
many hydrocarbon traps (Uko, Emudianughe, & Tamunobereton-ari, 2013).




Figure 28: Schematic cross section of the Anambra and Niger delta basins. The subsidence
of the dilated transitional crust has accommodated up to 10 kilometres of sediment
accumulation. Modified from (Uko, Emudianughe, & Tamunobereton-ari, 2013)

5.2           Single commodities

5.2.1 Gold (Au)

5.2.1.1 Location of occurrences

Currently, the Mineral occurrence database of Nigeria (MODB) contains 1604 gold
occurrences (Figure 29). These are mainly located in the Basement Complex and are almost
predominantly located within the schist belts.




                   Figure 29: Location of confirmed Gold (Au) occurrences in Nigeria.




Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 61
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
              MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
              Mining Investment Facilitation Project
              D6 Project Final Report                                                                   Project 20220074




5.2.1.2 Shape and interpreted type of occurrences

According to the appearance of the mine sites in airborne photographs and their geological
and topographical environment they can be distinguished into the following three types:

         Lenticular shape (1,092 points): open cast mine sites (pits) that follow mainly the recent
          drainage system, and, to a certain degree, paleo-terraces (Figure 30A). These
          occurrences are interpreted as placers.

         Vein, load, reef (469 points): mine sites are mainly shafts or linear deep channels
          following mainly the mineralization. They are interpreted as ‘Hydrothermal’, and
          correspond with fault systems/lineaments, ranging from a few tens of metres to a few
          kilometres (Figure 30B). All points of this classification have structural data (dip and
          direction). A wide variety of mineralization types are expected within this class, such as
          quartz veins and sulphide mineralizations, with and without carbonates.

         Stockworks / dome-like (disseminated, veinlets) (45 points): this type of mineralization
          is interpreted as ‘Hydrothermal’. However, the mineralisation is not located in single
          structures but in zones of various thickness, large extensions and related to intrusives.
          Despite some visible veins, the irregular pits do not reveal any main direction (Figure
          30C).




         250m                                    150m                                   350m

  type, the mine sites follow the recent river sediments, (B) Occurrence 4586, Name: ’EL-
         002962 - 13’ veins oriented N65/90 for around 900m and (C) Deposit 239, Name:
            ’Kampani/Rimi 1’, irregular cluster of pits, with smaller pits related to veins.

5.2.1.3 Genetic Model

The occurrences are associated with three predominant geological contexts:

        Sediments (Quaternary/Tertiary): alluvial (recent) and paleo-terraces (older) from
         existing sources, transported by water and concentrated on low-energy areas.

        Schist Belts: are linked with the tectonic events that created the perfect conduit for fluid
         flow and precipitation, thus are structurally controlled. The locations of gold deposits
         are not correlated to the type of country/host rock, however linked to pre-existing green-
         schist belts.

        Old Granites: mineralization occur in and/or around Syn- and Post-tectonic granites
         possibly related with subsequent shearing after the emplacement of these granites,
         accompanying the late Pan-African pegmatites (see 5.2.3), or as hydrothermal-
         metasomatic processes.



5.2.2 Tin (Sn)

5.2.2.1 Location of occurrences

Currently, 451 Sn occurrences across Nigeria were recorded and characterized in the MODB
(Figure 31). They are related exclusively to two metallogenic provinces:

        the rare metal-bearing pegmatite belt located in the Basement Complex (southwest
         area of the country) as a secondary mineral (discussed in 5.2.3), and

        The alkaline ring complexes (known as “Younger Granites” (located mainly in the
         Central basement domain and to a lesser degree in the South of the transitional tectonic
         domain This province, extending from northern Niger to south-central Nigeria, covers
         a total area of 7500 km2 and consists of massifs of over 50 anorogenic ring complexes
         made up of both volcanic and plutonic rocks formed during the Mesozoic (Kinnaird J. ,
         Hydrothermal alteration and mineralization of the alkaline anorogenic ring complexes
         of Nigeria, 1985).

5.2.2.2 Shape and interpreted type of occurrences

The tin occurrences in Nigeria can be divided into three main types of shapes:

        Lenticular (335 points) - These points, corresponding to placers, are characterized by
         an accumulation of minerals with a lateral extension much greater than its depth.
         Located close to their origin, are the result of in-situ weathering processes affecting
         primary tin lodes and granitic rocks. The mineralization type of this occurrences is
         "Sedimentary and diagenetic" (Figure 32). They are the predominant tin extraction type
         in the country (Lehmann, 1982).

5.2.2.3 Genetic Model

The tin ores are mainly contained in lode-bearing veins within cooling joints, fractures and
greisenized granites (Pastor, 1985) (Olade M. , 2020). According to (Kinnaird J. , Hydrothermal
alteration and mineralization of the alkaline anorogenic ring complexes of Nigeria, 1985). The
main two mechanisms understood to be responsible of mineralization of Sn, Zn, W, and Nb,
along with trace amounts of Cu, Fe, Bi, U, and REE are:

        Hydrothermal alteration (considered the main one),

        Sodic metasomatism, potash metasomatism,

        Chloritization and argillization.

5.2.3.4 Genetic Model

The emplacement of Pegmatites in Nigeria has been linked to the final stages of the Pan-
African Orogeny at around 553 to 555 Ma (Melcher, et al., 2015) under the following possible
theories:

        Possibly not emplaced during the main cooling phase of the magmatism of this period,
         but during the metasomatism events of the post-collisional stage (Melcher, et al., 2015),

        emplacement is far from their source allowing further fractionation times, however, they
         seem not to be linked to the Older Granites,

        Nigerian pegmatites have magmatic and post-magmatic origins (Ibrahim & Hayatu,
         2021).




Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:    Date changed:    Date printed:           Page 70
         release               x                      13/05/2024       24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




Pegmatites can be grouped into two main categories (Oyebamiji, et al., 2018):

        LCT: Lithium-caesium-tantalum bodies associated with S-type quartz rich granites, and

        NYF: Niobium-yttrium-fluorine bodies associated with A-type granites.



Mineralogy of the pegmatites is as follows (Melcher, et al., 2015):

        Ta–Nb pegmatites around Oke–Ogun field belong to the LCT family,

        Tantalite-(Mn) is more common at the Lema–Share–Ndeji field,

        Cassiterite-rich tantalite-(Fe) and stibiotantalite are concentrated in the Ijero and
         Ibadan–Oshogbo fields,

        Fe-rich CGM are associated with the Nasarawa–Keffi and Isanlu–Kabba fields

        Zinnwaldite in the Younger Granites area (Abaa, Kinnaird, Ike, & Turaki, 1984).

        An overview of the paragenesis of the resulting minerals can be seen in Figure 39,
         noting that spodumene postdates the lepidolite and they do not crystalize
         simultaneously with Ta and Nb.

5.2.4 Lead (Pb) and Zinc (Zn)

5.2.4.1 Location of occurrences

There are 359 points registered for lead and/or zinc (Figure 40). This project focuses only on
those deposits within the Benue Trough Domain where Pb and Zn occur as main commodities.
As secondary, associated or accessory, it occurs in:

        Salt/Brine in the ‘Lower Benue Trough’,

        Barite mainly in the ‘Middle Benue Trough’,

        Copper occurrences described along Benue Trough,



Further associations with other commodities outside the Benue Trough with Pb-Zn as
subordinate are:

        Gold in the Basement Complex, in Schist Belts at ‘Kebbi’ and in Migmatite-Gneiss
         Complex at ‘Baban Tsauni’

        Tin, Tantalum and Niobium in the Young Granites’.

5.2.4.3 Genetic Model

The evolution of the area (Figure 43) is calculated to have the following phases:

        Lower- Cretaceous (Aptian-Albian): when extensional tectonics commenced as a
         rifting branch of the dismemberment of Gondwana with marine and continental
         sedimentation.

        Upper Cretaceous (ca. 80 Ma): a shift to compression, paired with magmatism,
         controlled the area with a reduction on the sedimentation. Intense metamorphism in
         this phase produced the folding and faulting allowing the flow of fluids from the
         magmatic activity to reach the upper part and generate the fault-controlled deposits in
         the area.

        Upper Cretaceous - Palaeocene (ca. 70 – 60 Ma): end of metamorphic stage and no
         more documented occurrences are documented.

                                                                                                      Paralic sed.

                                                                                                      Tectonic phase

                                                                                                      Extension

                                                                                                      Transtension
                                                                                                      Compression
                                                                                                      Transpression
                                                                                                      Magmatism

                                                                                                      Metamorphism
                                                                                                      intensity

                                   Trough. Modified after (Benkehlil, 1989).

Zagai Wasagu and Sabon Tunga Wasagu) in the Western Basement Complex. In the mineral
occurrence database, there are 16 manganese points solely located in Kebbi State (Figure
44). Some reports identify some occurrences within the Young Granite suite which require
further analysis and, therefore, are not included here.

The mode of occurrence of these deposits has been defined as “Vein / Lode / Reef” and

southwest of Darena (points Darena 1 to Darena 14) and is characterized by some pits aligned
in the N140 direction (Figure 45).




                                                                         200m


                       Figure 45: Manganese deposit 191, Name “Darena – 1“.

5.2.5.3 Genetic Model

This deposit is described as hydrothermal with sedimentary influence, inside some meta-
sediments of the Basement Complex (Adamu, Obaje, Sidi, Aweda, & Liman, 2021). The
following relationships are interpreted responsible for mineralization:

        Initial metamorphosed sediments carrying Mn-garnets where altered and concentrated
         the Mn into other phases, such as spessartine and almandine.

        From a carbonaceous protolith, a supergene process altered the original phases and
         precipitated mineral-bearing Fe and Mn phases (Mücke, 2005).

5.2.6 Uranium (U)

5.2.6.1 Location of occurrences

Only a 9 occurrences have been discovered and information on their subsurface geological
setting and origin is scarce (Olade M. , 2020). Uranium is primarily located within igneous and
sedimentary rocks in six states Figure 46) within the following two Domains:

        Benue Trough,

        Eastern Basement Complex.




               Figure 46: Location of confirmed Uranium (U) occurrences in Nigeria.




Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 79
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




5.2.6.2 Shape and interpreted type of occurrences

Uranium occurrences in Nigeria can be distinguished into four types (Olade M. , 2020) in line
with the IAEA classification scheme (IAEA, 2018), which is based on their geological setting:

        Dissemination in peralkaline granites: anorogenic granites (Oshin, 1986) with presence
         of uraniferous pyrochlore with concentrations of up to 3.5 wt. % UO2 and up to 4.3 wt.
         % ThO2 (Beer, 1952), and up to 190 ppm of uranium content in arfvedsonite albite
         granite (Girei et al., 2019) and 7 wt. % (Ogunleye P. O., 2006).

        Vein-type mineralization in brecciated granites and rhyolites: major occurrences are
         concentrated in the Adamawa Massif hosting the Mika uranium mineralization with
         significant economic interest (Suh, 1998). Estimated uranium resources at Mika total
         52 tons at 0.63wt. % U with vertical extents of 130 meters, while at Ghumchi, 100 tons
         at 0.90wt. % U with vertical extents of 200 meters (Karniliyus & Akedegwa, 2018).

        Sandstone-hosted deposits: located in sediments of the Bima Formation in the Upper
         Benue Trough and in the Ezeaku Formation on Asu River Group and the Odukpani
         Formation in the Lower Benue Trough. Uranium minerals are disseminated or present
         as veinlets in brecciated sandstone at depth up to 200 meters, with 0.18 wt. % to 0.25
         wt. % of U concentration (Olade M. , 2020).

        Phosphorite (rock phosphate): sedimentary marine origin within the Sokoto basin and
         Dahomey basin (Obaje, 2014). Average uranium concentrations along the phosphate
         nodules and pellets range from 29-65 ppm (Ogunleye P. O., 2002) (Mustapha, 2022).



5.2.6.3 Genetic Model

The following mechanisms are proposed for the mineralization of Uranium in Nigeria:

        Mesozoic volcanic rocks are the primary source of uranium in Nigeria, or,

        Uranium metal may have been remobilised by hot fluids from Pan-African granites
         (Bute et al., 2020).

   Figure 47 provides a schematic interpretation of the processes that lead to the precipitation
   of the uranium along fracture zones of Pan-African orogeny in the volcanic-related U



Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 80
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




   deposits within the Eastern Nigeria Terrane (Bute et al., 2020). The vein-type
   mineralization model can be characterized by the emplacement of a volcanic body serving
   as a heat source for the mineralization fluid derived from granitic terrain. The fault
   structures functioned as channels and mechanical carriers, ultimately leading to the
   deposition of pitchblende. Surface and near-surface leaching of pitchblende resulted in the
   formation of secondary mineralization (Funtua, 1999).




     Figure 47: Schematic model section representing geological setting in relation to U
    remobilization and enrichment along the fault rocks, modified after (Bute et al., 2020).



According to the 1983 IUREP report (IAEA, 1983) speculative resources are estimated in the
range 10,000–50,000 tons U, hosted in both sandstone and vein-type environments (in the
Older Granites). Conventional uranium resources are further estimated at 152 tons, located in
Mika and Ghumchi vein-type deposits associated to granites, featuring grades of 0.63% and
0.90% U, respectively (Karniliyus & Akedegwa, 2018).

The shape of these occurrences is of “Stockwork / domlike (disseminated, veinlets)”, as seen

                                                  650m                                       600m


Figure 49: A) Chromium in serpentinite at occurrence 356, Name: Tungan Kudaku, within the
 “serpentinite belt” to the west of the Anka Schist Belt. B) Occurrence 3690, Name: Mallam
                                         Tanko of disseminated chromite.



5.2.7.3 Genetic Model

Although scarce information exists from these deposits in Nigeria, a correlation with the well-
studied deposits in Brazil is possible. The process of formation of these occurrences is believed
to have followed these steps:

         Active subduction zone with oceanic rocks,

         Close of subduction zone and slab roll-back,

         Volatilization of the underlying ultramafics emplaced new sills onto the overlying crust,
          allowing the formation of Ni-Cr rich globules,

         Further deformation along alteration in the following Periods within the shear zones
          affected these rocks, producing the observable serpentinized bodies.

5.2.8.3 Genetic Model

There are two main geological times when iron deposits have formed:

        Pan African Age: The work by (Mücke, 2005) and (Turner D. , 1983) identified a deep
         water basin sedimentary origin (possibly Eburnean), that during the Kibaran cycle
         (Turner D. , 1983) and the Pan-African pahse, concentrating the magnetite ore as
         hematite and specularite under metamorphic conditions along, in some cases, with
         Manganese phases. The schematic view of this process is seen in Figure 53. In many
         cases, where Older Granites intruded, contact metamorphism recrystallized the
         hematite into magnetite as retrograde reaction. This type of genesis correlates with the
         ores found in Brazil, like the extractive activities by the Minereste Company in Seridó,
         geologically linked to Nigeria.

        Cretaceous: Mainly found in the Agbaja and Enugu areas, have their origin in alluvial
         deposition as high-energy shallow-marine environment ooids (Olade M. , 2020). These
         deposits tend to form large extensions of plateaus due to their depositional
         environment, showing no deformational features and a lateritic crust (Figure 54).

5.2.9.3 Genetic Model

The formation of these deposits is identified to be under this stages:

        Bioclastic source related to gypsum accumulation (Abimbola, Olatunji, Akande, &
         Borode, 2005).

        Sediment deposition during the Cretaceous with a combination of high-energy
         environment and reworking of indurated apatite muds (Figure 57) under oxic conditions
         (Adekeye & Akande, 2004).

        Further weathering due to humid conditions during the Paleocene enriched the
         concentration of the original nodules.

The probable reserves of phosphates in Nigeria range in the millions of tones. However,

(Olowofela, Okeyode, & Idowu, 2019).

5.3           Metallogeny

The metallogeny of Nigeria is a compilation of several mechanisms and events determined by
the geological tectonic development of the country as described in chapter 5.1



5.3.1 Schist Belts

The schist belts in Nigeria are correlated to the accretion of several different terranes with
island arc affinity, amalgamating to the West of the Nigerian Shield and creating a densely
packed faulted area. This concept can be summarized in Figure 58. A schematic view of the
configuration of the layers can be seen in Figure 59, containing greenstone belts, the
migmatite-gneiss complex and ultramafic intrusives. Related deposits are structure related,
stratiform, and disseminated, containing stockwork type sulphides and native gold.




          Figure 58: Accretion of terranes and generating further fault zones (swarms).




 Figure 59: Schematic representation of the geology during the Paleoproterozoic-Archean.



Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 92
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




5.3.2 Pan-African Orogenesis

The final phase of deformation and activity in the basement complex is linked to the intrusion
of the Older Granites. Their age ranges between 750-450Ma and are almost entirely barren
(Woakes, Rahama, & Ajibade, 1987). However, these bodies are responsible for the majority
of the known mineral occurrences in the Basement Complex. Cross-cutting all the pre-existing
rocks, these granites probably remobilized previous mineralizations. Toward the latter part of
this period, pegmatites and gold deposits are identified and are restricted within the shear
zones. Related commodities are: Au, Li, Sn, Ta, Nb, gemstones, and secondary Pb and Zn
(Figure 60).




           Figure 60: Schematic representation of the intrusion of the Older Granites.



5.3.3 Jurassic Magmatism

This stage is mainly represented by the intrusion of Young Granites into the Basement
Complex, marking a geological reactivation after the pause since the Pan-African Orogeny.
These intrusives are discordant high-level intrusions which succeed the extensive acid
volcanics and are emplaced into the Precambrian to early Palaeozoic basement (Kinnaird,
Bowden, Ixer, & Odling, 1985). These intrusions cut through all pre-existing lithologies and are
linked to the upwelling of the crust do to a regional extension event pre Gondwana break-up




Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 93
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




(Figure 61). This period marks the formation of alkaline complex related mineralizations: Li,
Sn, Ta, Nb, W, Mo and REE.




    Figure 61: Schema of the anorogenic intrusions (Young Granites) into the Basement
                                                      Complex.



5.3.4 Cretaceous Rifting and Benue Trough Basin

Further extension commenced with normal faulting of the crust followed by a rift zone (the
Benue Trough). Mantle upwelling meant further magmatism, fracturing and folding in the area.
Hydrothermal fluids permeated the upper sediments/rocks and precipitated commodity related
deposits (also possibly re-distributed earlier deposits) as shown in Figure 62. Further activity
formed the U-hosted sandstones through infiltration of porous sediments (e.g., sandstones).




Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 94
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                    Project 20220074




   Figure 62: Schematic representation of the rifting of the Benue Trough and respective
                                                      sedimentation.



5.3.5 Cenozoic Sediments

Further sedimentation, erosion and tropical weathering (e.g., laterites) shaped the landscape
of Nigeria during the Cenozoic (Figure 63). The most important deposits here are terraces,
recent alluvial placers (e.g., Au and Ta-Nb) and residual mineralization in laterites (e.g., Fe
and P). Further basaltic volcanism is observed towards the norther part of the Benue Trough.

6.             Mineral Potential Mapping

6.1            Methodology

The identification of prospective areas consists of the following steps (see Figure 64):

         Collection of spatially accurate base data (as possible), including but not limited to:

              o     Mineral occurrence data, covering important properties of the commodities:

                            genetic type,

                            size and,

                            other important parameters

              o     Geological maps

              o     Tectonic and other relevant geoscientific maps

              o     Geophysical datasets

              o     Geochemical datasets

              o     Remote sensing data

              o     Exploration models (for identification of potentially mineralisation controlling
                    factors)

         Base data processing:

              o     extraction of mineralisation controlling parameters from the base data, and

              o     calculation of derivatives of the raw and extracted data

         Data integration and analysis, using:

              o     None supervised and

              o     Supervised approaches and algorithms

The unsupervised approaches identify relationships between the different datasets (e.g.
cluster and factor analysis). The results of unsupervised approaches can be used as input
parameters for supervised approaches. In the supervised approach, the locations of known
mineral occurrences are used to train AI algorithms (e.g. artificial neural networks) to identify



Status    draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 98
          release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




sites with similar properties as those which have been used to train the algorithm. The
calculations create maps showing favourabilities on a scale between 0 (not favourable) and 1
(very favourable) for the identification of occurrences of the searched commodity.




Figure 64: Methodology for the usage of Machine Learning for the identification of favourable
                                   areas for selected commodities in Nigeria.




Status   draft                      Version 1.0       Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:          Page 99
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




A deep understanding of mineral occurrence formation processes is necessary to extract the
important controlling parameters from the available datasets.

The mineral systems approach concept states that the formation of a mineral deposit requires
a series of processes and events, which are expressions of larger earth systems existing at
different scales and times (McCuaig, Beresford, & Hronsky, 2010). This approach allows the
flexibility of a common analysis over different mineral systems, constrained by data at all
scales, leading to new discoveries with different styles instead of analogue deposits.




Figure 65: Minerals system concept with the main processes required for a mineral deposit to
                   be formed. Modified after (Geological Survey of Finland, 2020).



In order to use the concept shown in Figure 65 for creation of input datasets for ANN based
exploration targeting, the fundamental processes must be translated into mapable criteria
extracted from available datasets. As stated by (McCuaig, Beresford, & Hronsky, 2010), the
following successive steps are needed:



Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 100
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




   1. Recognise the critical processes of the mineral system.

   2. What are the constituent processes of the system?

   3. Ascertain the geological expressions of the system as targeting elements.

   4. Identify the mapable criteria that allow the detection of those targeting elements.



In this project, we use exclusively the hybrid modelling: a combination of knowledge driven
and data driven approaches (. We combine the knowledge from the mineral system analysis
expressed in exploration models with the ANN based data driven approach.




Figure 66: Relationship between knowledge driven and data driven methods

         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
              MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
              Mining Investment Facilitation Project
              D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




6.2            Single commodities

6.2.1 Gold (Au)

According to current knowledge, in Nigeria the following three genetic types of Au occurrences
(see also chapter 5.2.1) exist:

         Gold related to metamorphic belts,

         Granite related gold occurrences,

         Placers.

For each genetic type a separate Mineral Predictive Map (MPM) was calculated.



6.2.1.1 Gold related to Metamorphic Belts (file: Nigeria_AuLiTa_100m, model # 31)

For mineral potential mapping of gold in metamorphic belts the following frame conditions were
applied:

         Model application area: The Western Domain

         Mineral occurrence points: exact and verified locations of 369 Au occurrences in schist
          belts, without spatial relation to post kinematic granitic intrusives



As controlling parameters for ANN calculations, derivatives of the following datasets have been
used:

         Geological datasets  The litho-structural map of Nigeria (NGSA, 2022):

              o     Shear zones (early and late)

              o     lithologies of metamorphic rocks

              o     Terrain boundaries

         Geophysical datasets:

              o     Processed airborne magnetic data (Fugro, 2009), resolution 125 m

              o     Processed airborne radiometric data (Fugro, 2009), resolution 125 m



Status    draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 102
          release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




The following dataset was used as input:

        Training data: the respective Au-occurrences have been buffered with a radius of 200
         m creating circles of 400 m diameter simulating the area of the supposed mineralised
         area.

For the ANN model, the following parameters have been used:

        Number of iterations: 100

        Number of hidden layers: 1

        Balancing: 50:50

        Number of input neurons: 22

        Number of output neurons: 1

        Number of neurons in the hidden layer: 67

        Number of connections: 1541



The model quality and reliability can be evaluated by learning curve and the histograms of the
favourability values of all data points compared with the known mineral occurrence (the “yes”
points) data points (Figure 67). The “yes” point favourability value data histogram ideally shows
the maximum towards the right side (close to te value 1). In the given case the maximum is
between 0.7 and 0.8, indicating the heterogeneity in the data, possibly to:

        wrong labelling of mineralization type, being actually, for instance, a placer deposit and
         not a metamorphic belt related deposit,

        Different genetic history of the mineral occurrences,

        Wrong registered commodity / gold is not the main commodity,

        Other reasons (e.g.: the used data do not sufficiently describe the mineralisation,
         uncertainties of the geological borders…)



With further collection of correct data, the output of the ML algorithm with provide better results.

7.            Target Selection and Ranking
For the selection of targets, specific criteria has been selected for each corresponding
commodity to allow the identification of the most prospective areas. The two highest ranked
targets for each group have been selected and further explained as key prospective areas.
Nonetheless, the remaining targets are by no means less relevant and shall not be ignored,
mainly ranking lower due to lack of knowledge. Further exploration is recommended to
increase geological knowledge and de-risk the areas for interested parties.

7.1           Gold (Au) Targets

7.1.1 Ranking and Location

7.1.1.1 Criteria Catalogue

From the general Au Favourability Map (combination of Figure 68 and Figure 70), a selection
of single targets was selected and recommended for further evaluation. This selection
represents, to the current knowledge and understanding of the Consultant, the most
prospective sites along with a set of typical cases within the target areas.

The selected target areas were ranked using the following catalogue of criteria:

        MPM shape and intensity:

                           MPM pattern and favourability value (FV)            Score
                           Compact pattern and FV > 0.9                          5
                           Compact pattern and FV > 0.8                          4
                           Compact pattern and FV > 0.7                          3
                           Diffuse pattern and FV > 0.9                          2
                           Diffuse pattern and FV > 0.8                          1

        Within schist belt:

                                           Within schist belt          Score
                                                 Yes                     5
                                                  No                     1

        Within ultramafic complex:

                                                Yes                      5

7.1.2 Description of single targets

Only the highest ranked Targets are selected and described more into detail. They are Target
Gold 1 (Au 1: Ife-Ilesha), Target Gold 2 (Au 2: Kimu), and Target Gold 8 (Au 8: Pategi).

7.1.2.1 Target Gold 1

7.1.2.1.1 Summary

Located between the towns of Ilesha and Ife-Ife in Osun State, the target contains at least 17
known occurrences of Au, related to hydrothermal veins, and over 100 placer deposits, all
hosted in Na and Ns rocks. Presently, all mining activities are artisanal and active. Favourability
mapping suggests a diffuse shape with values exceeding 0.8. The total area of this target is
699.37 km2.



7.1.2.1.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.1.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a very diffuse overall shape with a total coverage of about 40%,
composed of several smaller continuous profiles with values over 0.8 (Figure 86). Correlation
is clear with hard-rock deposits and with some placer deposits.



7.1.2.1.2.2 History

Mining activities in the area are only observable on placer deposits starting sometime around
2016 with minor ravines or gullies affected. By the end of 2020, the impact in the area is evident
extending to river valleys but has remained artisanal. By 2023, all major active sites are fully
developed, however, no main source of the body (hard-rock) has been definitely reported.



7.1.2.1.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the Ifewara Schist Belt part of a regional mobile belt crossing Nigeria
from North to South. The rocks are composed of early- to mid-Neoproterozoic mafic to
ultramafic gneisses interlayered with meta-volcanosediments including psammitic to



Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 131
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




psammopelitic schists (Na) along with greenschist to amphibolite facies clastic and volcanic
sequences (Ns) and within 4 major shear zones. Bordering to the West, migmatites and
anatectic granites indicate the Archean to Paleoproterozoic (Neoproterozoic reworked)
Western Basement Complex (Pmg).

7.1.2.1.2.4 Mineralization

The NIMEP project has identified several soil anomalies towards this areas with N-S to NNE-
SSW trends. Au values are low suggesting the source is concealed. When compared with the
RTP signal of the area, a series of anomalies can be correlated with the traced sources.
Mineralization in the area is interpreted as hydrothermal veins with substantial amount of
placer gold deposits (>100). Drainage pathways and DEM indicate the existence of nearby
gold sources that may extend over the entire length of the proposed target area. The selected
extent contains at least 17 known locations of hard-rock mineral occurrences divided into three
main clusters.

            Mining is distributed on topographic lows like gullies, ravines and river valleys
             (Figure 85),

            All mining activities in the area are artisanal and remain active,

            Production in the area has been reported from 26 up to 123.319 ounces of gold.

7.1.2.2 Target Gold 2

7.1.2.2.1 Summary

Located close to Bin Yauri, Kebbi State, the target contains at least 101 active hard-rock
deposits of Au of hydrothermal genesis within Ns and Nlg rocks. Presently, all mining activities
are artisanal and active. Favourability mapping suggests a diffuse shape with values
exceeding 0.8. The total area of this target is 287.47 km2.



7.1.2.2.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.2.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map (superimposing favourability of gold in granites and metamorphic belts)
shows a very compact overall shape with a total coverage of about 80% (Figure 91).
Correlation is clear with hard-rock deposits.



7.1.2.2.2.2 History

Some sporadic mine trenches are visible in satellite imagery from 2009 followed by a rapid
extension during 2017 and partial abandonment (seasonal reasons?). Peak activities are
observed during 2022 after which pits remain active with some expansion towards the east.



7.1.2.2.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the Upper Anka Shear zone part of a regional mobile belt crossing
Nigeria in a NE-SW trend. The rocks are composed of early- to mid-Neoproterozoic
greenschist to amphibolite facies clastic and volcanic sequences (Ns) and within 3 major shear
structures and sandwiched between two Neoproterozoic to Cambrian late- to syn-tectonic
granitoids including leucogranites, porphyritic granites and micro granites (Nlg).

7.1.2.2.2.4 Mineralization

The NIMEP project indicated some close by area with up to 8 g/t along with ultramafic bodies.
By means of the magnetic signal, the intrusives can be distinguished and several “pressure
shadows” can be identified, possibly as a product of the regional shear and rotation of the
inelastic behaviour of the granites. Mineralization in the area is interpreted as hydrothermal
veins related with related diagenetic placer deposits. More than 101 active mines on veins and
a number of placer deposit mines are documented in this target. The veins follow a SW-NE
trend (Figure 89) and are grouped into two clusters, one of them with an approximate area of
10 km2.

            Mining is mainly done on veins with a NE-SW trend (Figure 85),

            All mining activities in the area are artisanal and remain active,

            No production reports are available from this site.

7.1.2.3 Target Gold 3

7.1.2.3.1 Summary

Located close to Kusheriki in Niger State, the target is devoid of any documented hard-rock
mineral occurrences and only contains 2 placer deposits, all hosted in Ns and close to Nog
rocks. Favourability mapping suggests a diffuse shape with values exceeding 0.8 highlighting
significant potential for further exploration and development. The total area of this target is
482.37 km2.



7.1.2.3.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.3.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map (superimposing favourability of gold in granites and metamorphic belts)
shows an intermediate between compact and diffuse shape with values averaging 0.7, with
some cases reaching to 0.97, and covering up to 80% of the whole area (Figure 93).



7.1.2.3.2.2 History

Currently, there are only exploration licenses without any main area of mining and no main
work area, suggesting it remains underexplored and with potential.



7.1.2.3.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the Ifewara - Birnin Wari Shear Zone part of a regional mobile belt
crossing Nigeria from North to South in a NNE-SSW trend. The rocks are composed of early-
to mid-Neoproterozoic greenschist to amphibolite facies clastic and volcanic sequences (Ns)
and lie within 3 major shear structures. Sandwiched between, Neoproterozoic to Cambrian
syn-tectonic granitic plutons and batholiths (Nog) are mapped. Partial coverage of
geochemistry throws high values of Barium (ca. 1250 ppm) linked with hydrothermal ore veins.
These anomalies can be correlated with the response found in the Radiometric Potassium and
aligns well with the DEM of the area suggesting two dilational jogs product of the shear zone
and allowing the emplacement of the Older Granites.

observable starting 2023.

7.1.2.4 Target Gold 4

7.1.2.4.1 Summary

Located to the southeast of New Bussa, Niger State, the target contains at least 27 active hard-
rock deposits of Au of hydrothermal genesis within Ns and Nlg rocks. Presently, all mining
activities are artisanal and active under a mining lease. Favourability mapping suggests a
compact and continuous shape with values exceeding 0.8. The total area of this target is
333.34 km2.



7.1.2.4.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.4.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map (superimposing favourability of gold in granites and metamorphic belts)
shows a very compact and continuous shape with values above 0.8 with a total coverage of
about 90% (Figure 95). Correlation is clear with hard-rock deposits.



7.1.2.4.2.2 History

Activities in the area only started around 2012 with two mall pits of no more than 80m. Imagery
from 2016 indicates additional activities to the NW but seem all but active. Only towards 2019,
activities picked up pace with several additional workings following the trend of the initial pits.



7.1.2.4.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the south-east of the Anka Shear Zone part of a regional mobile belt
crossing Nigeria in a NE-SW trend. The rocks are composed of early- to mid-Neoproterozoic
greenschist to amphibolite facies clastic and volcanic sequences (Ns) along Neoproterozoic to
Cambrian late- to syn-tectonic granitoids including leucogranites, porphyritic granites and
micro granites (Nlg) and syn-tectonic granitic plutons and batholiths (Nog). Magnetic signal in
the area suggests some bodies trending NW-SE that suggest a control on mineral
occurrences.

7.1.2.5 Target Gold 5

7.1.2.5.1 Summary

Located north of Dogon Daji, Zamfara State, the target contains at least 21 active hard-rock
deposits of Au of hydrothermal genesis hosted on Ns rocks. Presently, all mining activities are
artisanal and active, and are partially covered by a mining lease. Favourability mapping
suggests a compact continuous shape shapes with values exceeding 0.8. The total area of
this target is 410.25 km2.



7.1.2.5.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.5.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map (superimposing favourability of gold in granites and metamorphic belts)
shows a sectioned map with 4 compact shapes (with values above 0.8) covering a total of 70%
of the target (Figure 97). Relative good correlation with documented hard-rock deposits.



7.1.2.5.2.2 History

Activities in the area only started around 2012 with two mall pits of no more than 80m. Imagery
from 2016 indicates additional activities to the NW but seem all but active. Only towards 2019,
activities picked up pace with several additional workings following the trend of the initial pits.



7.1.2.5.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the northern part of the Anka Shear Zone part of a regional mobile belt
crossing Nigeria in a NE-SW trend. The rocks are composed almost exclusively of early- to
mid-Neoproterozoic greenschist to amphibolite facies clastic and volcanic sequences (Ns) with
small intrusions of Neoproterozoic to Cambrian syn-tectonic granitic plutons and batholiths
(Nog). Magnetic signal in the area indicates a continuous structure trending NE-SW following
the local geology.

7.1.2.6 Target Gold 6

7.1.2.6.1 Summary

Located west of Bagega town, Zamfara State, the target contains at least 15 active hard-rock
deposits of Au of hydrothermal genesis hosted on Ns rocks. Presently, all mining activities are
artisanal and active following an N-S mineralization trend, and are covered by a mining lease.
Favourability mapping indicates a continuous shape with values averaging 0.7 on three main
spots. The total area of this target is 334.95 km2.



7.1.2.6.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.6.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map (superimposing favourability of gold in granites and metamorphic belts)
shows 3 compact shapes (with average values above 0.7) covering a total of 50% of the target
(Figure 99), and showing relative good correlation with all but one documented hard-rock
deposits.



7.1.2.6.2.2 History

Mining in the area seem to have only commenced sometime during 2011 with a small cluster
of artisanal pits to the north of the target. The sites seem to have been abandoned and only
until 2023 renewed events are perceptible again, with 3 main trenches (one up to 100m) aimed
to understand the spatial thickness of the local mining cluster. From images of 2019, it is
evident the exploration in the area expanded dramatically including at least two new mining
areas (around 1 km away from the original site) and more than 15 new trenches. Recent
imagery (2023) and valid mining leases indicate that mining has expanded both north and
south following the local geology.



7.1.2.6.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the northern part of the Anka Shear Zone part of a regional mobile belt
crossing Nigeria in a NE-SW trend. The rocks are composed almost exclusively of early- to



Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 155
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




mid-Neoproterozoic greenschist to amphibolite facies clastic and volcanic sequences (Ns) with
small intrusions of Neoproterozoic to Cambrian syn-tectonic granitic plutons and batholiths
(Nog) and bordered to the right by migmatites and anatectic granites indicate the Archean to
Paleoproterozoic (Neoproterozoic reworked) Western Basement Complex (Pmg). Three main
late shear planes run N-S through the target that correlate perfectly with the magnetic signal.

7.1.2.6.2.4 Mineralization

Mineralization in the area is interpreted as hydrothermal veins related to metamorphic belts.
Due to the distribution of the sites, a stockwork deposit may be suggested. A total of 15
hydrothermal veins are documented in the area. The principal direction of the veins is N-S
structurally/geologically controlled, as suggested by the magnetic signal (Figure 98), with some
minor conjugate NE-SW conjugate bodies, grouped into one main cluster with an area of 9,31
km2.

            Mining is mainly done on veins with a N-S trend,

            All mining activities in the area are artisanal (unclear which remain active),

            No production reports are available from this site.



7.1.2.6.3 Prospect area

        Length of the main structure: 920m (interpreted)

        Number of occurrences: 14 hard-rock

        Type of mining: artisanal

7.1.2.7 Target Gold 7

7.1.2.7.1 Summary

Located east of Birni-Gwari town, between Kaduna and Niger States, the target contains at
least two active hard rock deposits of Au part of a massive hydrothermal vein system
(stockwork) hosted in Ns rocks. Presently, all mining activities are artisanal and active following
an N-S mineralization trend, and are covered by a mining lease. Favourability mapping
indicates a diffuse shape with values averaging 0.7 on two main spots. The total area of this
target is 410.28 km2.



7.1.2.7.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.7.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map (superimposing favourability of gold in granites and metamorphic belts)
is a very diffuse shape only covering 20% of the target (Figure 101), however, a more regional
view indicates this area is part of a set of compact figures. Values over 0.8 correlate well with
existing mineral occurrences.



7.1.2.7.2.2 History

Mining in the area seem to have only commenced sometime during 2011 with a small cluster
of artisanal pits to the north of the target. The sites seem to have been abandoned and only
until 2023 renewed events are perceptible again, with 3 main trenches (one up to 100m) aimed
to understand the spatial thickness of the local mining cluster. From images of 2019, it is
evident the exploration in the area expanded dramatically including at least two new mining
areas (around 1 km away from the original site) and more than 15 new trenches. Recent
imagery (2023) and valid mining leases indicate that mining has expanded both north and
south following the local geology.

7.1.2.7.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the northern part of the Ifewara – Birnin Guari Shear Zone part of a
regional mobile belt crossing Nigeria in a NE-SW trend. The rocks are composed almost
exclusively of early- to mid-Neoproterozoic greenschist to amphibolite facies clastic and
volcanic sequences (Ns) with small intrusions of Neoproterozoic to Cambrian syn-tectonic
granitic plutons and batholiths (Nog). One main late shear plane runs N-S through the target
and correlates perfectly with the magnetic signal.



7.1.2.7.2.4 Mineralization

Mineralization in the area is interpreted as hydrothermal veins related to metamorphic belts.
Due to the distribution of the sites, a stockwork deposit may be suggested. A total of 2
hydrothermal veins are documented in the area. The principal direction of the veins is N-S
(Figure 100) and structurally/geologically controlled, as suggested by the magnetic signal and
the geology, grouped into one main cluster with an area of 15,18 km2.

            Mining is mainly done on veins with a N-S trend following a topographic low,

            All mining activities in the area are artisanal (all seem to be active),

            No production reports are available from this site.

            Structures can be followed up to 13 km within a 600 m band.

        Average width: 7m

        Thickness of cluster: 200m

        Number of occurrences: 2 hard-rock

        Type of mining: artisanal

7.1.2.8 Target Gold 8

7.1.2.8.1 Summary

Located south of Pategi, in Kwara State, the target contains at least 99 active hard-rock
deposits of Au suggesting two massive hydrothermal vein systems (stockwork) hosted in Pbg
rocks. Presently, all mining activities are artisanal and active following an NE-SW
mineralization trend, and are covered by several mining licenses. Favourability mapping
indicates a compact shape with values over 0.8 on two main provinces. The total area of this
target is 1,446.02 km2.



7.1.2.8.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.8.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map (superimposing favourability of gold in granites and metamorphic belts)
is a very compact shape covering over 60% of the target (Figure 106), with two main shapes
(one on the eastern part, another on the western part) suggesting two different provinces.
Values over 0.8 correlate well with existing mineral occurrences.



7.1.2.8.2.2 History

Mining in the area seem to have only commenced sometime during 2011 with a small cluster
of artisanal pits to the north of the target. The sites seem to have been abandoned and only
until 2023 renewed events are perceptible again, with 3 main trenches (one up to 100m) aimed
to understand the spatial thickness of the local mining cluster. From images of 2019, it is
evident the exploration in the area expanded dramatically including at least two new mining
areas (around 1 km away from the original site) and more than 15 new trenches. Recent
imagery (2023) and valid mining leases indicate that mining has expanded both north and
south following the local geology.

7.1.2.8.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the central area of the Western Basement Complex with at least three
well defined early Shear-zones. The rocks are composed of Archean to Paleoproterozoic
(Neoproterozoic reworked) domains of coherent suites of gneisses with limited disruption
attributed to anataxis and migmatisation (Pbg) with slivers of migmatites to anatectic granites
commonly comprising enclaves of relict gneiss (Pmg) and small intrusions of Neoproterozoic
to Cambrian syn-tectonic granitic plutons and batholiths (Nog).
