7.1.2.8.2.4 Mineralization

Mineralization in the area is interpreted as hydrothermal veins related to metamorphic belts.
Due to the distribution of the sites, a stockwork deposit may be suggested. 99 known hard-
rock deposits have been identified, separated into and east section with an area of 169,94 km2,
and a western section with 96,34 km2. The principal direction of the veins is NE-SW (Figure
104). The analysis of the magnetic signal (especially the RTP DX derivative) shows several
NE-SW trending structures.

            Mining is mainly done on veins with a NE-SW trend following a topographic low,

            All mining activities in the area are artisanal (all seem to be active),

            No production reports for Au are available from this area.

7.1.2.9 Target Gold 9

7.1.2.9.1 Summary

Located around the towns of Maikendi and Runtu Fulani, in Kebbi State, the target contains at
least 67 mineral occurrences of Au suggesting a stockwork deposit hosted in Nlg rocks.
Presently, all mining activities are artisanal and active following an NE-SW mineralization
trend, and are only partially covered by licenses. Favourability mapping indicates a compact
shape with values over 0.8 on two main provinces. The total area of this target is 269.93 km2.



7.1.2.9.2 Characterization of the target

7.1.2.9.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map (superimposing favourability of gold in granites and metamorphic belts)
has two very compact shapes covering over 60% of the target (Figure 108), one very rounded
to the SE of the target, the second an elongated continuous province. Values over 0.9 correlate
very well with existing mineral occurrences.



7.1.2.9.2.2 History

From the available imagery, already by 2013 some pits and shafts from artisanal miners
existed and apparently were abandoned (towards the central part of the target). Only until
2017, the northern section (NE of the intrusive body) started experiencing activity from miners
which seem to have remained active since. By 2022, some abandoned central sites seem to
have been reactivated, and further activities expanded further north and south towards the
other two intrusive bodies.



7.1.2.9.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the north-west area of the Western Basement Complex. The rocks are
composed of Archean to Paleoproterozoic (Neoproterozoic reworked) domains of coherent
suites of gneisses with limited disruption attributed to anataxis and migmatisation (Pbg) and
three main intrusive bodies of Neoproterozoic to Cambrian late- to syn-tectonic granitoids



Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 170
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




including leucogranites, porphyritic granites and micro granites (Nlg). The whole target area is
enclosed between one late and one early shear zone.



7.1.2.9.2.4 Mineralization

Mineralization in the area is interpreted as hydrothermal veins related to metamorphic belts.
Due to the distribution of the sites, a stockwork deposit may be suggested. The area has 67
known occurrences of hard-rock gold deposits and are spatially linked with the intrusions within
a 27,07 km2 area. The main cluster of occurrences in the middle intrusion follow a SW-NE
trend seemingly radiating from the centre, however, the active sites to the south-western side
of the Granite seem to bend around it (Figure 107). The analysis of the magnetic signal (RTP
2VD) shows several NE-SW trending structures suggesting a control on the mineralization.

            Mining is mainly done on veins with a NE-SW trend,

            Activities are concentrated in an apparent contact metamorphism halo,

            All mining activities in the area are artisanal (all seem to be active),

            No production reports for Au are available from this area.



7.1.2.9.3 Prospect area

The area of interest of 7,40km2 contains the following characteristics:

        Length of the main structure: 350m

        Average width: 7m

        Thickness of cluster: 2.000m

        Number of occurrences: 23 hard-rock

        Type of mining: artisanal

        Status: active

7.2.2 Description of single targets

7.2.2.1 Target Tin 1

7.2.2.1.1 Summary

Located in the Kerifa Hills of Plateau State, the target area boasts six occurrences of Sn-Nb
within supergene enriched sediments, aligned along east-west structures within the alkaline
ring complex. Presently, all mining activities in the region are artisanal, with no active licenses
reported as of 2023, although Topwide Ventures Ltd had previously conducted exploration in
the area. Favourability mapping suggests a cohesive shape with values exceeding 0.9. The
total area of this target is 434.95 km2.



7.2.2.1.2 Characterization of the target

7.2.2.1.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a very compact shape with values over 0.9 covering around 60%
of the target (Figure 112) and correlating with the known Sn-Nb occurrences.



7.2.2.1.2.2 History

Activities in the area can be traced back at least to 2003 where several sites were visible
through satellite imagery in artisanal scale. Around 2017, activities reached their maximum
with at least 4 main sites, followed by a reduction in visible changes to just some sporadic
cases.



7.2.2.1.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of undifferentiated felsic to mafic alkaline volcanics including flood
basalts (Jv) and undifferentiated alkaline granites predominantly biotite granite with
subordinate arfvedsonite granite and Fayalite granite (Jg). The complex is called Sha-Kaleri.

7.2.2.2 Target Tin 2

7.2.2.2.1 Summary

The target area situated near Barkin Ladi town in Plateau State, close to Jos, boasts 11
occurrences of Sn-Nb within supergene enriched sediments, aligned along east-west
structures within the alkaline ring complex. Presently, all mining operations in the vicinity are
artisanal, with no active licenses reported as of 2023, although Topwide Ventures Ltd had
previously conducted exploration in the area. Favourability mapping reveals a compact shape
with values surpassing 0.9. The total area of this target is 412.61 km2.



7.2.2.2.2 Characterization of the target

7.2.2.2.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a very compact shape with values over 0.9 covering around 80%
of the target (Figure 115) and correlating with the known Sn-Nb occurrences.



7.2.2.2.2.2 History

Activities in the area started in colonial times and can be seen in satellite imagery dating back
to 1985. No major pit or industrial activity has surfaced since then with only some new small-
scale sites visible after 2017, most of which are placer deposits close to the major abandoned
pits. Main activities seem to concentrate towards the south-west of the target.



7.2.2.2.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of undifferentiated felsic to mafic alkaline volcanics including flood
basalts (Jv) and undifferentiated alkaline granites predominantly biotite granite with
subordinate arfvedsonite granite and Fayalite granite (Jg). The complex is called Ropp and
has intruded older migmatitic gneisses (Pmg).

some coincide with abandoned extraction pits, allowing the identification of further mining
activities. The identified mineralization in the area is Pneumatolytic producing veins/lodes that

            Placer deposits is the main target of mining in the area,

            Around 17 tons of Tin concentrate are reported to be produced per year.

7.2.2.3 Target Tin 3

7.2.2.3.1 Summary

The target area situated in Pankshin, Plateau State, does not currently host any known hard-
rock occurrences of Sn-Nb, but it does possess identified placer deposits. Mining activities in
the region are predominantly artisanal, with two recent sites showing renewed interest, albeit
all active licenses are exploratory in nature. Favourability mapping indicates a cohesive shape
with values exceeding 0.9. The total area of this target is 331.63 km2.



7.2.2.3.2 Characterization of the target

7.2.2.3.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a very compact shape closely following the existing geology. The
observed values are over 0.9 covering around 60% of the target (Figure 117).



7.2.2.3.2.2 History

Although no clear evidence is seen in satellite imagery, activities in this area started already
by 1990s without any posterior noticeable uptake in happenings, except 2 small sites around
2023 (unclear from available information if they are indeed mining activities).



7.2.2.3.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of undifferentiated felsic to mafic alkaline volcanics including flood
basalts (Jv) and undifferentiated alkaline granites predominantly biotite granite with
subordinate arfvedsonite granite and Fayalite granite (Jg). The complex is called Sara-Fier.



7.2.2.3.2.4 Mineralization

No hard-rock mineral occurrence has been reported inside the selected target. However, at
least 4 placer deposits are documented within the ring complex. The target is located in the
area of the Younger Granites identified as alkaline intrusives and classified as ring complexes.

7.2.2.4 Target Tin 4

7.2.2.4.1 Summary

The target area situated near Dutsen Daji in Plateau State hosts just one known hard-rock
occurrence, aligning with a north-south trend within the alkaline ring complex. Presently, all
mining operations in the vicinity are artisanal, with I.G.I. Global Investment Ltd holding the
license for a prospective area. Favourability mapping reveals a cohesive shape with values
exceeding 0.9. The total area of this target is 429.01 km2.



7.2.2.4.2 Characterization of the target

7.2.2.4.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a very compact shape with values over 0.9 covering around 50%
of the target (Figure 119) and correlating with the local geology.



7.2.2.4.2.2 History

No major pit or industrial activity can be spotted in the expanse of the target suggesting mining
has not really kick-started in the area. Some spots do suggest artisanal miners may have
worked prior 2020s, however, only until 2023 evidence of mining is observed.



7.2.2.4.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the area of the Younger Granites identified as alkaline intrusives and
classified as ring complexes. The rocks are composed of undifferentiated felsic to mafic
alkaline volcanics including flood basalts (Jv) and undifferentiated alkaline granites
predominantly biotite granite with subordinate arfvedsonite granite and Fayalite granite (Jg).
The complex is called Jarawa and has intruded older migmatitic gneisses (Pmg).

7.2.2.5 Target Tin 5

7.2.2.5.1 Summary

The target area situated around Tibchi in Bauchi State boasts at least four known hard-rock
occurrences along with several placer deposits. Pneumatolytic veins within the alkaline ring
complex follow an NNW-SSE trend. Presently, all mining activities in the region are artisanal,
and as of November 2023, no valid license has been issued for this area (the previous license,
held by Eso Terra Investment Ltd, has expired). Favourability mapping illustrates a compact
shape with values surpassing 0.9. The total area of this target is 339.14 km2.



7.2.2.5.2 Characterization of the target

7.2.2.5.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows two very compact shapes with values over 0.9 covering around
50% of the target (Figure 123) and correlating with known Sn-Nb mineral occurrences.



7.2.2.5.2.2 History

Placer deposits towards the western flank of the target can be traced back to have started
sometime before 1985 with further expansion in towards the end of 2019. However, no hard-
rock mine site can be spotted until 2023 with at least 5 sites popping-up towards the centre of
the target. No industrial workings are spotted and no industrial development can be seen.



7.2.2.5.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the area of the Younger Granites identified as alkaline intrusives and
classified as ring complexes. The rocks are composed of undifferentiated alkaline granites
predominantly biotite granite with subordinate arfvedsonite granite and Fayalite granite (Jg).
The complex is called Tibchi and has intruded older migmatitic gneisses (Pmg).

7.2.2.6 Target Tin 6

7.2.2.6.1 Summary

The target area situated around Ombi in Nasarawa State is characterized by one known hard-
rock occurrence and one substantial placer deposit, which includes tantalum. Structures within
the area appear to follow an NE-SW trend within the alkaline ring complex. Presently, all mining
activities except for the significant placer deposit, which is controlled by Kenyang Mining
Company Limited, are artisanal. Favourability mapping reveals a compact elongated shape
with values exceeding 0.9. The total area of this target is 289.72 km2.



7.2.2.6.2 Characterization of the target

7.2.2.6.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a very compact and elongated shape with values over 0.9
covering around 80% of the target (Figure 125) and correlating with one known Sn-Nb mineral
occurrence and the geology.



7.2.2.6.2.2 History

The main placer mine is probably linked to colonial times (pits can be spotted in satellite
imagery of 1985) but remained inactive until sometime around 2012 when activities resumed.
Only in this area machines and trucks are visible, suggesting an industrial process along with
a central compound (processing?). All other sites in this target remain exploited by artisanal
workers.



7.2.2.6.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the area of the Younger Granites identified as alkaline intrusives and
classified as ring complexes. The rocks are composed of undifferentiated alkaline granites
predominantly biotite granite with subordinate arfvedsonite granite and Fayalite granite (Jg).
The complex is called Afu.

7.2.2.7 Target Tin 7

7.2.2.7.1 Summary

The target area situated around Ririwai in Kano State boasts a minimum of four known hard-
rock occurrences, characterized as pneumatolytic veins alongside diagenetic placer deposits.
The magnetic anomaly in the area is low (RTP) within the alkaline ring complex. Primary mining
operations in the region are industrial, with some additional artisanal sites. Favourability
mapping depicts a compact rounded shape with values exceeding 0.9. The total area of this
target is 301.02 km2.



7.2.2.7.2 Characterization of the target

7.2.2.7.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a very compact and elongated shape with values over 0.9
covering around 50% of the target (Figure 127) and correlating with one known Sn-Nb mineral
occurrence and the geology.



7.2.2.7.2.2 History

The area has been worked since colonial times (pits can be spotted in satellite imagery from
1985) but remained barely active before 2020. Sometime between 2021 and 2023 and
expansion of mining activities can be seen, transforming from artisanal to industrial with the
addition of heavy-duty machinery, compounds and trucks, followed by clear tailing mounds.
Nonetheless, areas outside the central area show hefty artisanal workings.



7.2.2.7.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the area of the Younger Granites identified as alkaline intrusives and
classified as ring complexes. The rocks are composed of undifferentiated felsic to mafic
alkaline volcanics including flood basalts (Jv) and undifferentiated alkaline granites
predominantly biotite granite with subordinate arfvedsonite granite and Fayalite granite (Jg).

The complex is called Jarawa and has intruded older migmatitic gneisses (Pmg). The complex
is called Riruwai.

7.2.2.7.2.4 Mineralization

At least four main hard-rock deposits are documented following a NW-SE tending mineral lode.
Several placer deposits mine the overlying sediments with supergene tin mineralization. The
identified mineralization in the area is mainly pneumatolytic lodes with diagenetic placer
deposits. RTP magnetic signal indicates a strong magnetic-low area and corresponding with
the last phase of the intrusives of the Younger Granites. By further inspection of the satellite
imagery, new mining activities (starting on the second half of 2023) are spotted supporting the
potential of this area for additional resources.

            Main mine site is a on lode deposits and apparently exploited industrially, including
             trucks and heavy machinery (Figure 126), and underground,

            Artisanal workings populate the area, number of spots increased dramatically
             starting 2019,

            No production data is available from this site.

7.2.2.8 Target Tin 8

7.2.2.8.1 Summary

The target area situated around Rishi in Bauchi State boasts a minimum of six known hard-
rock occurrences, characterized as pneumatolytic veins alongside diagenetic placer deposits.
The magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization controlled by geological structures. Primary
mining operations in the region are artisanal, particularly focused on placer deposits. As of
November 2023, the main mining site lacks a license owner. Favourability mapping illustrates
a compact rounded shape with values surpassing 0.9. The total area of this target is 222.39
km2.



7.2.2.8.2 Characterization of the target

7.2.2.8.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows two compact areas with values over 0.95 for the NW and over
0.85 for the SE covering around 60% of the target (Figure 129) and correlating with almost all
known Sn-Nb mineral occurrences and the geology.



7.2.2.8.2.2 History

The area has been worked since colonial times (pits can be spotted in satellite imagery from
1985) but remained barely active before 2020. Sometime between 2021 and 2023 and
expansion of mining activities can be seen, transforming from artisanal to industrial with the
addition of heavy-duty machinery, compounds and trucks, followed by clear tailing mounds.
Nonetheless, areas outside the central area show hefty artisanal workings.



7.2.2.8.2.3 Geological Setting

The target is located in the area of the Younger Granites identified as alkaline intrusives and
classified as ring complexes. The rocks are composed of undifferentiated felsic to mafic
alkaline volcanics including flood basalts (Jv) and undifferentiated alkaline granites
predominantly biotite granite with subordinate arfvedsonite granite and Fayalite granite (Jg).

             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




The complex is called Jarawa and has intruded older migmatitic gneisses (Pmg). The complex
is called Tongolo.



7.2.2.8.2.4 Mineralization

Over six known Pneumatolytic mineral occurrences are documented, some reaching up to 700
m in length, along with eight placer deposits pinpointing to several potential sources. The
identified mineralization in the area is mainly pneumatolytic lodes with diagenetic placer
deposits. The north-western section shows a low-magnetic region (RTP) that comprises all
deposits and shows resemblance with other Younger Granites mineralized areas. On close
inspection, some new unknown hard-rock workings were identified.

            Main mine site is a on lode deposits and apparently exploited industrially, including
             trucks and heavy machinery (Figure 128), and underground,

            Artisanal workings populate the area, number of spots increased dramatically
             starting 2019,

            No production data is available from this site.

7.3.2 Description of single targets

7.3.2.1 Target Pegmatite 1

7.3.2.1.1 Summary

The target area situated around Keffi in Nasarawa State containing 17 pegmatitic veins hosted
in gneisses. The magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization geologically and structurally
controlled. Primary mining operations in the region are artisanal. Currently, there area is
covered both by mining leases and exploration licenses. Favourability mapping illustrates a
compact elongated shape with values over 0.9. The total area of this target is 403.24 km2.



7.3.2.1.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.1.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows one compact elongated area (some blur to the north) with values
over 0.9 and covering around 80% of the target and correlating well with known Li-Ta pegmatite
occurrences (Figure 135).



7.3.2.1.2.2 History

The area has been worked since colonial times (pits can be spotted in satellite imagery from
1985) but remained barely active before 2020. Sometime between 2021 and 2023 and
expansion of mining activities can be seen, transforming from artisanal to industrial with the
addition of heavy-duty machinery, compounds and trucks, followed by clear tailing mounds.
Nonetheless, areas outside the central area show hefty artisanal workings.



7.3.2.1.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) and undifferentiated banded felsic gneisses,
subordinate leucogneiss layers, mafic gneisses and paragneisses (Pbg), within the transitional
tectonic domain. One main early shear zone runs N-S.

7.3.2.1.2.4 Mineralization

Over 17 known pegmatite occurrences are documented sitting all on topographic highs, with
one body interpreted to run for more than 1.5km in length. The identified mineralization in the
area is pegmatitic veins intruding into older rocks. The magnetic signal (RTP 2VD) indicates
there is a main magnetic body with NNW-SSE trend that can be spatially correlated with the
documented deposits. The whole cluster of pegmatites are within an area of about 70 km2.

            NIMEP project identified:

                   o   body thickness of 20m (drilling),

                   o   Li2O values up to 1.56% and up to 527ppm Ta,

            Rubidium and Tantalum stream sediment anomalies,

            All mine sites but one seem to be exploited by artisanal miners (Figure 134),

            Production of Lithium in the area has been reported to be between 60 to 280t per
             annum.

7.3.2.2 Target Pegmatite 2

7.3.2.2.1 Summary

The target area situated around Keffi in Nasarawa State containing 12 pegmatitic veins hosted
in gneisses. The magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization is structurally controlled. Mining
in the region is artisanal and concentrates in 2 main areas with bodies of at least 20m
thickness. Currently, there area is covered both by mining leases and exploration licenses.
Favourability mapping illustrates a diffuse shape with several independent bodies with values
over 0.8. The total area of this target is 438.21 km2.



7.3.2.2.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.2.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a diffuse shape made up of several independent continuous
shapes, suggesting favourability follows a possible NNE-SSW pegmatite trend of several
independent bodies. Although covering less than 40% of the target selected, values over 0.8
correlate well with documented mineral occurrences (Figure 139).



7.3.2.2.2.2 History

Satellite imagery records from 2005 already indicated that the area was mined by then, with at
least 2 sites showing minor artisanal workings in shafts. By 2014, one site rapidly developed
one major pit suggesting machinery was used, but became abandoned by 2016. In 2017,
another main site southwards developed into the main mine area with a mixture of shafts and
pits in two main pegmatite bodies. Further sites appeared the following years, all but one have
artisanal workings while the exception indicates the usage of machinery for the opening of pits.
Up to 2023, only records of three areas indicate active sites.



7.3.2.2.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) and undifferentiated banded felsic gneisses,



Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 226
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




subordinate leucogneiss layers, mafic gneisses and paragneisses (Pbg), within the transitional
tectonic domain. Two main Neoproterozoic to Cambrian late-tectonic granitoids have been
interpreted to have intruded the area. Two main early shear zones run N-S.

7.3.2.2.2.4 Mineralization

12 known pegmatite occurrences are documented (with additional ones currently unregistered)

The identified mineralization in the area is pegmatitic veins intruding into older rocks. The

correlating well with the documented occurrences and suggesting a structural control of the
mineralized bodies. The whole cluster of pegmatites, are within an area of about 40 km2.

            All mine sites but one seem to be exploited by artisanal miners (Figure 138),

            Production of Lithium in the area has been reported to be 169t per annum.

7.3.2.3 Target Pegmatite 3

7.3.2.3.1 Summary

The target area situated Close to Atabo town, Oyo State, containing 9 pegmatitic veins hosted
in gneisses close to granitic intrusions. The magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization is
structurally controlled. Mining in the region is artisanal but is mainly abandoned except one
main pit. Currently, there area is covered by a mining lease. Favourability illustrates a diffuse
but elongated area with values over 0.8. The total area of this target is 284.50 km2.



7.3.2.3.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.3.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows indicates a diffuse shape but suggesting a continuous and
elongated favourable region with similar characteristics (Figure 141). All documented mineral
occurrences fall within values over 0.9 and correlate well with this shape.



7.3.2.3.2.2 History

Satellite imagery is only available starting 2011 and already shows at least 2 main mining sites
with well-developed pits (e.g., NR-Komu 1) suggesting machinery, and some other artisanal
areas with several shafts. By 2018, new artisanal sites appeared with their distinctive shafts,
while other sites became abandoned (e.g., one of the original major sites). By 2018, another
main site had opened showing machinery activities and a distinct pit. Last available images
from 2020 as well as current data from MI and MCO indicate areas remained active until today.



7.3.2.3.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) and undifferentiated banded felsic gneisses,
subordinate leucogneiss layers, mafic gneisses and paragneisses (Pbg), within the Western
Basement Complex. Two main Neoproterozoic to Cambrian late-tectonic granitoids have been

7.3.2.4 Target Pegmatite 4

7.3.2.4.1 Summary

The target area situated around south-east of Ibadan, Oyo State, containing no known lithium
or tantalum pegmatitic veins, but several aquamarine sites within gneisses and close to a
granitic intrusion. The magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization is probably structurally
controlled. Mining in the region is artisanal aided with excavator machinery. Currently, there
area is partially covered by mining leases. Favourability mapping illustrates a diffuse elongated
shape with several independent bodies with values over 0.8. The total area of this target is
475.35 km2.



7.3.2.4.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.4.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows two shapes roughly compact and continuous suggesting two
main favourable areas almost entirely limited to Pbg rocks. Approximately 50% of the target
selected is covered by values over 0.8 where, although no Li-Ta pegmatites are documented,
correlate well with at least two known aquamarine and tourmaline artisanal mines (Figure 143).



7.3.2.4.2.2 History

Activities, according to satellite imagery, started mainly as exploitation mainly for granite some
time before 2014, with only one small aquamarine site containing 3 small shallow pits. This
initial site saw constant development through the years, joined in 2017 by another aquamarine
site to the south east of the local older granite. By 2019, several other sites popped-up in the
area suggesting more mineralized bodies. By 2022, all sites seemed to remain active but in an
artisanal level.



7.3.2.4.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) and undifferentiated banded felsic gneisses,



Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 236
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




subordinate leucogneiss layers, mafic gneisses and paragneisses (Pbg), within the Western
Basement Complex. One Neoproterozoic to Cambrian late-tectonic granitoid has been
interpreted to have intruded the area (Nlg). Several late shear zones run N-S and encompass
some interpreted greenschist to amphibolite facies sequences (Ns).



7.3.2.4.2.4 Mineralization

No known pegmatite mineralized with lithium or tantalum is documented in the area, however
at least 2 known mines for pegmatite related gemstones exist (e.g., tourmaline, aquamarine).
The identified mineralization in the area is pegmatitic veins intruding into older rocks. The
magnetic signal (RTP 2VD) shows one long structure to the west of the target where
favourability is high is seemingly delimited by two shear zones, while the second suggests a
relationship with the intruded Older Granite (Nlg).

            Caesium and Tantalum stream sediment anomalies,

            Known pegmatite-related gemstone mines in the area (Figure 142),

            No production information in the area is available.

        Average width: 50m (interpreted)

        Number of occurrences: None currently registered

7.3.2.5 Target Pegmatite 5

7.3.2.5.1 Summary

The target area situated around Saki East in Oyo State, containing two known mineralized
pegmatitic veins within gneisses close to granitic intrusions. The magnetic anomaly suggests
mineralization is probably structurally controlled. Mining in the region is solely artisanal.
Currently, there area is partially covered by one mining lease. Favourability map has a diffuse
elongated shape with values over 0.8. The total area of this target is 575.27 km2.



7.3.2.5.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.5.2.1 Favourability

The shape of the favourability map indicates one main continuous shape with a very diffuse
nature and following roughly the regional shear zone. Approximately 50% of the target selected
is covered by values over 0.6 where the only two mineral occurrences documented are roughly
contained (Figure 145).



7.3.2.5.2.2 History

Satellite imagery indicates that mining in the area had started sometime before 2012 with
already countless visible shafts by that year in the length of around 1.700m, many seemingly
abandoned with only around 400m of active shafts. Between 2018 and 2021, the second
pegmatite site operated, but was then abandoned. By 2022, the major site saw a reduction
and concentration of its activities within only 200m.



7.3.2.5.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) within the Western Basement Complex. Two
Neoproterozoic to Cambrian late-tectonic granitoids are interpreted, one of which has
considerable regional size (Nlg). Only one late shear zone running N-S has been mapped in
the area.

Two known mineralized pegmatites are documented in the area. The identified mineralization
in the area is pegmatitic veins intruding into older rocks. The magnetic signal (RTP 2VD)
indicated a continuous rock body running N-S that in several places is interpreted to be cross-
cut by NE-SW trending structures (faults?) concentrated on the southern part of the target,
while the northern section suggest a deformed area between the two Nlg granitoids.

            Caesium and Tantalum stream sediment anomalies,

            Two known mineralized pegmatites in the area (Figure 144),

            A total of 4928t of tourmaline are reported to be produced per annum.

        Type of mining: artisanal

7.3.2.6 Target Pegmatite 6

7.3.2.6.1 Summary

The target area situated around Dami Dami town, Niger State, containing no known
mineralized pegmatitic veins but a few considerable mining activities related to gold and linked
with pegmatites exist. Hoisting rocks are gneisses and volcano-sedimentary greenschist to
amphibolite facies sequences. The magnetic anomaly suggests there are structural controls
on the area. Currently, there are exploration and mining leases for gold in the area.
Favourability map has a diffuse elongated shape with values over 0.8.



7.3.2.6.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.6.2.1 Favourability

The shape of the favourability map indicates two main continuous diffused shapes, separating
the north and south in two interpreted provinces. Approximately 40% of the target selected is
covered by values over 0.6 (Figure 147). The total area of this target is 315.67 km2.



7.3.2.6.2.2 History

Although somewhat unclear, the satellite imagery suggests that the area had already been
active by 2013 with minor shafts and placer pits related to gold mining. By 2019, the activities
had moved slightly to the north, but remained artisanal with some evidence of hard-rock
workings. One year later (2020), mining had expanded dramatically in all directions and at
least 3 pits of around 50m average length appeared. In 2024, all areas seemed to remain
active with two more new main sites, one showing workings on hard rock with an artisanal pit
of 120m length while the second showing an industrial capacity with several trucks and
machinery on a 250m length pit.



7.3.2.6.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) and undifferentiated banded felsic gneisses,

         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024

             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




subordinate leucogneiss layers, mafic gneisses and paragneisses (Pbg), within the Western
Basement Complex. Several early shear zones run NE-SW and separate greenschist to
amphibolite facies sequences (Ns) in the area.



7.3.2.6.2.4 Mineralization

No known mineralized pegmatites are documented in the area, but mineralization from existing
gold mining can be interpreted either as pegmatitic veins intruding into older rocks or a
stockwork system. The stream network and the DEM suggest that the source of Caesium and
Tantalum anomalies is nearby. Through the magnetic signal (RTP 2VD) the shear zones/faults
are evident and indicate one major NNE-SSW structure to which the favourability correlates
well, and suggest influence from the intruding Older Granite in the vicinity.

            Caesium and Tantalum stream sediment anomalies,

            Au mineralized areas that may be linked to pegmatites within a 5,5km2 area (Figure
             146),

            No values of production reported in the area.

        Type of mining: artisanal

7.3.2.7 Target Pegmatite 7

7.3.2.7.1 Summary

The target area situated around Beni Hill, Niger State, contains no known mineralized
pegmatitic veins but has recent artisanal activities in an interpreted 2km body within gneisses.
The magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization is probably structurally controlled. Currently,
no mining license covers the suggested prospect, but several exploration licenses are in the
vicinity. Favourability map has a diffuse elongated shape with values over 0.8. The total area
of this target is 863.77 km2.



7.3.2.7.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.7.2.1 Favourability

The shape of the favourability map is very diffuse but indicates two main elongated trends that
follow the mapped shear zones and can be interpreted regionally as a general continuous
shape covering all the selected target (Figure 149). Values above 0.6 approximately cover
40% of the total selected region.



7.3.2.7.2.2 History

The start of the activities in the area can be interpreted not to be so far from the beginning of
2019 since a few mine shafts can be identified in the area. From that date onwards, the
activities increased dramatically showing several pits but remained in an artisanal level.
Further expansion of the activities continued into the 2022 remaining the main mine site of all
the target.



7.3.2.7.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) and undifferentiated banded felsic gneisses,
subordinate leucogneiss layers, mafic gneisses and paragneisses (Pbg), within the Western
Basement Complex and bordering the Transitional Zone. One Neoproterozoic to Cambrian

No known lithium-tantalum mineralized pegmatites are documented in the area. The identified
mineralization in the area is pegmatitic veins intruding into older rocks. The magnetic signal
(RTP 2VD) clearly indicates that the area is strongly structurally controlled with two continuous
bodies interpreted to follow an N-S trend and defining the shape/location of the highest
favourability values.

pegmatitic veins with proven thicknesses of 50m and hosted by gneisses closely related to the
local batholitic body. The magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization is probably structurally
controlled. Mining in the region is solely artisanal and extends for several kilometres. Currently,

compact region to the north east (Figure 151). Values above 0.8 approximately cover 70% of
the total selected region and correlate well with the documented pegmatitic mineral
occurrences.



7.3.2.8.2.2 History

The start of the activities in the area can be interpreted not to be so far from the beginning of
2019 since a few mine shafts can be identified in the area. From that date onwards, the
activities increased dramatically showing several pits but remained in an artisanal level.
Further expansion of the activities continued into the 2022 remaining the main mine site of all
the target.



7.3.2.8.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) within the Western Basement Complex. One syn-
tectonic granitic pluton/batholitic body (Nog) and one late-tectonic granitoid (Nlg) both of

7.3.2.9 Target Pegmatite 9

7.3.2.9.1 Summary

The target area situated around Danba Lema, Kwara State, containing 11 known mineralized
pegmatitic veins with proven thicknesses of 40m hosted by gneisses and batholith bodies. The
magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization is probably structurally controlled. Mining in the
region is solely artisanal and extends for several kilometres. Currently, there area is partially
covered by one exploration license. Favourability map has two compact rounded shapes with
values over 0.8. The total area of this target is 345.75 km2.



7.3.2.9.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.9.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows two very compact and rounded shapes suggesting two
independent favourable areas (Figure 153). The western favourable area seems to continue
further towards the NW. Values above 0.8 approximately cover 50% of the total selected region
and correlate well with the documented pegmatitic mineral occurrences.



7.3.2.9.2.2 History

By 2008, the Danba Lema area was already a known extraction site for artisanal miners with
two separate active areas separated by a few hundred meters. Barely two years later had the
expansion of the activities considerably changed, suggesting the action of excavator
machinery for the generation of not-so-shallow pits adding a third mining site. By 2021, this
further continued to the south. Additional sites appeared by this time to the north-west of the
target as artisanal sites with shafts.



7.3.2.9.2.3 Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) within the Western Basement Complex. One syn-
tectonic granitic pluton/batholitic body (Nog) of Neoproterozoic to Cambrian age are

         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024

             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




interpreted to have intruded the area. At least one main late and one early shears zones are
identified and run NE-SW just left of the Ifewara – Birnin Gwari Shear Zone.



7.3.2.9.2.4 Mineralization

At least 11 pegmatite deposits are documented in this area with the majority being currently
active. There are two main pegmatite bodies currently mined, one with an approximate visible
length of 1.2 km, a second pegmatite with around 800 m length. Magnetic signal of the area
suggests a considerable high magnitude anomaly around the deposits to the East, whereas a
low magnitude one (demagnetization?) towards the ones to the west.

            NIMEP project identified:

                   o   body thickness of 40m (drilling - Figure 152),

                   o   Li2O values up to 1.39% and up to 1005ppm Ta,

            All mining activities are artisanal

            No production values are available from this location.

        Length of the main structure: 1.000m (interpreted)

7.3.2.10 Target Pegmatite 10

7.3.2.10.1 Summary

The target area situated around Ila-Orangun, Kwara State, containing 3 known mineralized
pegmatitic veins, of which two are recent and active, one is abandoned, all hosted by gneisses.
The magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization is probably structurally controlled within
gneisses. Mining in the region is solely artisanal. Currently, there area has no claimed license.
Favourability map has one compact elongated shape with values over 0.8. The total area of
this target is 240.72 km2.



7.3.2.10.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.10.2.1       Favourability

The favourability map shows one elongated NE-SW trending compact shape (Figure 155).
Values above 0.8 approximately cover 80% of the total selected region and correlate well with
the documented pegmatitic mineral occurrences.



7.3.2.10.2.2       History

Mining activities in the area only started around 2013 with one open pit mine with around 100m
length. The structure and thickness of this site suggests machinery was probably used.
However, two years later (2015) it seems to have been abandoned and giving way at the end
of 2016 to a newer open pit mine some tens of meters to the south-east (NR-Mine 2). The
latter mine seems to have been also abandoned by 2018, and only until 2022 did two new
shallow workings appeared to the south-west (NR-Mine 3 and NR-Mine 4).



7.3.2.10.2.3       Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) and undifferentiated banded felsic gneisses,
subordinate leucogneiss layers, mafic gneisses and paragneisses (Pbg), within the Western




Status   draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 260
         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
             MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




Basement Complex. One main early extensional shear zone runs NE-SW through the middle
of the target.



7.3.2.10.2.4       Mineralization

This target contains at least three mine workings with only two remaining active sites. The
abandoned site is seen filled with water and has a deep pit. Magnetic anomalies trending SW-
NE correlate with the mineral occurrences. On inspection of the satellite imagery of the area,
further artisanal activities are spotted to the south of the target.

            Activities are artisanal and shallow, except for the abandoned site (Figure 154),

            Pegmatites seem to have a NE-SW trend,

            No production values are available from this location.



7.3.2.10.3 Prospect areas

The prospect area of 1,52km2 contains the following characteristics:

        Length of the main structure: 1.400m (interpreted)

        Average width: 40m (interpreted)

        Number of occurrences: 3

        Type of mining: artisanal

        Status: partially active

7.3.2.11 Target Pegmatite 11

7.3.2.11.1 Summary

The target area situated around Sepeteri Area, Oyo State, containing 12 known mineralized
pegmatitic veins hosted in gneisses, are all active and being exploited by artisanal miners. The
magnetic anomaly suggests mineralization is probably structurally controlled. Currently, there
area is covered by several mining leases. Favourability map has one oval diffused shape with
values reaching 0.8. The total area of this target is 245.14 km2.



7.3.2.11.2 Characterization of the target

7.3.2.11.2.1       Favourability

The favourability map shows a very diffuse oval shape with the longest axis oriented N-S trend
(Figure 157). The western favourable area seems to continue further towards the NW. Values
above 0.6 approximately cover 40% of the total selected region, and correlation with the
mineral occurrences is very low but interpreted to be a missing part of the overall regional
shape probably cut out by the presence of a national park.



7.3.2.11.2.2       History

By 2008, the Danba Lema area was already a known extraction site for artisanal miners with
two separate active areas separated by a few hundred meters. Barely two years later had the
expansion of the activities considerably changed, suggesting the action of excavator
machinery for the generation of not-so-shallow pits adding a third mining site. By 2021, this
further continued to the south. Additional sites appeared by this time to the north-west of the
target as artisanal sites with shafts.



7.3.2.11.2.3       Geological Setting

The rocks are composed of a mixture of Archean to Paleoproterozoic migmatitic gneisses with
relict mafic to felsic gneiss enclaves (Pmg) within the Western Basement Complex. At least
one main early shear zone runs throughout just right of the Anka Shear Zone.

7.3.2.11.2.4       Mineralization

Within this target and with values above 0.8, a cluster of known mineralized pegmatites (12
entries) concentrate in around 30 km2. All points are active operations. As seen in Figure 156,
mining in the area is extensive and probably expands further around. The magnetic signal in
the area suggests that a SE-NW tending anomaly may be correlated with the existing deposits
and suggests that the deposits to the NW can be linked with the ones in the SE.

            All mining activities are artisanal,

            No production values are available from this location.

        Length of the main structure: 1.8780m (interpreted)

        Average width: 700m (interpreted)

        Number of occurrences: 6

        Type of mining: artisanal

        Status: active

7.4.2 Description of single targets

7.4.2.1 Target Pb-Zn 1

7.4.2.1.1 Summary

The target within the Lower Benue Trough boasts 15 occurrences of Pb-Zn, two of which are
undergoing advanced activities and production, with an output exceeding 50kt of material per
year, owned by First Patriot LTD. Extensive N-S trending veins are prevalent, hosted within
interbedded argillaceous and sandstone beds. The favourability map reveals a compact shape
with values exceeding 0.8. The total area of this target is 999.34 km2.



7.4.2.1.2 Characterization of the target

7.4.2.1.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a compact shape with values over 0.8 for at least 80% of the
target (Figure 162) correlating with the known Pb-Zn occurrences. Holes (values below 0.6)
correlate with the hinge of synclines.



7.4.2.1.2.2 History

Activities in the area can be traced back at least to 2007, with an uptake in 2015.



7.4.2.1.2.3 Geological Setting

The regional rocks are identified as early Cretaceous and belong mainly to the Asu River
Formation (Car), composed of interbedded argillaceous and sandstone beds, and minimally to
the Eze Aku/Yolde Formations (Cey), containing carbonaceous shales, sandstones and
limestones.

         release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024

             Mining Investment Facilitation Project
             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




7.4.2.1.2.4 Mineralization

At least 15 known deposits are located inside this target. The target is located within the Lower
Benue Trough and covers two main and several minor anticlines and at least one syncline, all
trending SW-NE. The magnetic response in the area shows a high, possibly related to an
intrusive body linked with the source of the transported fluids to this area. The area contains
only vein occurrences with an interpreted hydrothermal genesis.

            One area has workings panning over 2.000m in length(Figure 160),

            Recent workings (mostly small-scale) on the majority of sites,

            A large active oval open-pit mine with heavy machinery (Figure 161),

            The main orientation of the exploited veins tends N-S,

            Reported mineral production amounts to 50kt of material per year.

        Average width of pits: 30m

        Number of occurrences: 5

        Status: active

7.4.2.2 Target Pb-Zn 2

7.4.2.2.1 Summary

The target within the Lower Benue Trough comprises 13 occurrences of Pb-Zn, with recent
activities observed, including one extending over 700 meters. The general vein trend follows
an NW-SE orientation and is hosted within early Cretaceous interbedded argillaceous and
sandstone beds. The favourability map indicates a diffuse shape with values over 0.8. The
total area of this target is 855.02 km2.



7.4.2.2.2 Characterization of the target

7.4.2.2.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a diffused favourability with values over 0.8 covering around 50%
of the area. Correlation with Pb-Zn occurrences on the southern section following a continuous
shape along the mapped regional fault (Figure 163).



7.4.2.2.2.2 History

Activities in the area started around 2015 with recent (2022) extension towards the southern
border of the target.



7.4.2.2.2.3 Geological Setting

The regional rocks are identified as early Cretaceous as an intercalation of the Asu River
Formation (Car), composed of interbedded argillaceous and sandstone beds, and the Eze
Aku/Yolde Formations (Cey), containing carbonaceous shales, sandstones and limestones. A
volcanic source (1VD) is spotted and the mapped fault structures on the Oban Massif can be
interpreted to continue northwards towards the location (or close by) to the known deposits.

             D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




7.4.2.2.2.4 Mineralization

At least 13 known deposits are located inside this target, all of which are located towards the
SE border of the target. The target is located within the Lower Benue Trough and encompasses
what is interpreted as a series of anticlines and synclines trending SW-NE. The area contains
only vein occurrences with an interpreted hydrothermal genesis and solely located within the
Car formation.

            The longest structure mined goes for at least 700m (Figure 160),

            No heavy machinery or infrastructure is identified,

            Barite production on the south of the target is reported with 1.170 t per year,

            The main orientation of the exploited veins tends NW-SE.

7.4.2.3 Target Pb-Zn 3

7.4.2.3.1 Summary

The target within the Middle Benue Trough encompasses 10 occurrences of Pb-Zn, including
one site with active underground mining operations and a substantial processing complex,
processing over 20kt of material per year, under the ownership of Synee Alumony Mining
Limited. Extensive N-S trending veins, some spanning over 2 kilometers, are prevalent,
accompanied by minor E-W trending bodies. These veins are hosted within late Cretaceous
syn-rift sandstones interbedded with limestones along the core of the Keana anticline. The
favorability map depicts a compact shape with values exceeding 0.9. The total area of this
target is 303.60 km2.



7.4.2.3.2 Characterization of the target

7.4.2.3.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a compact shape following the length of the target and covering
around 90% of the selected area (Figure 168). Values over 0.9 correlate with the known Pb-
Zn occurrences and some barite occurrences.



7.4.2.3.2.2 History

Activities in the area can be traced back at least to 2010 with two main districts. An industrial
complex has been constructed (around 2019) next to one deposit revitalizing the mining
activities, including trucks, processing machinery, tailings and, possibly, subterranean
workings.



7.4.2.3.2.3 Geological Setting

The regional rocks are identified as Late Cretaceous and syn-rift as Keana/Awe Formations,
composed of sandstones, shales and siltstones (Cka1) in the centre of the anticline followed
by cross-bedded fine- to very-coarse sandstones interbedded with limestones (Cka2) towards
the sides of the fold.

7.4.2.3.2.4 Mineralization

At least 10 known deposits of Lead-Zinc and 6 of Barite are located inside this target. The
target is located within the Middle Benue Trough. The area lies exactly on the core of the
Keana anticline, a major SW-NE trending fold structure. The magnetic response suggests an
intrusive body (interpreted as sills) that may be linked with the source of the transported fluids
to this area. Type of mineral genesis is interpreted as hydrothermal and occurs as vein-like
deposits.

            One area has workings panning over 2.000m in length,

            A large open-pit mine with apparent sub-surface workings, including heavy
             machinery (Figure 166).

            The main orientation of the exploited veins tends N-S, with minor E-W bodies.

            Reported mineral production amounts to 20kt of material per year.

7.4.2.4 Target Pb-Zn 4

7.4.2.4.1 Summary

The target within the Middle Benue Trough comprises 10 occurrences of Pb-Zn, among which
one site is actively engaged in underground mining operations, featuring a large processing
complex processing over 20kt of material annually, under the ownership of Synee Alumony
Mining Limited. Extensive N-S trending veins, some stretching beyond 2 kilometres, are
prevalent, along with minor E-W trending bodies. These veins are hosted within late
Cretaceous syn-rift sandstones interbedded with limestones along the core of the Keana
anticline. The favourability map indicates a compact shape with values exceeding 0.9. The
total area of this target is 381.45 km2.



7.4.2.4.2 Characterization of the target

7.4.2.4.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a compact shape following the length of the target and covering
more than 90% of the selected area (Figure 170). Values over 0.9 correlate with the known
Pb-Zn occurrences and some barite occurrences.



7.4.2.4.2.2 History

Mining activities in the area can be traced back at least to 2010 with some abandoned pits.
Sometime around 2018, several mines picked up renewed activities in very small scale, with
just one particular site showing continuous activities to date. No industrial machinery is seen
in the area.



7.4.2.4.2.3 Geological Setting

The regional rocks are identified as Late Cretaceous made up of syn-rift as Keana/Awe
Formations, composed of cross-bedded fine- to very-coarse sandstones interbedded with
limestones (Cka2), and the Eze Aku/Yolde Formations, composed of carbonaceous shales,
limestones and fine to medium grained sandstone (Cey).

anomalies. Type of mineral genesis is interpreted as hydrothermal and occurs as vein-like
deposits.

7.4.2.5 Target Pb-Zn 5

7.4.2.5.1 Summary

The target within the Middle Benue Trough boasts 30 occurrences of Pb-Zn, with three primary
structures currently being exploited by artisanal miners. The license holder for this area is H &
H Mines Ltd. The predominant trend of the mineralized bodies extends E-W for over 1.5
kilometres, with additional minor bodies trending N-S. These occurrences are hosted within
late Cretaceous syn-rift carbonaceous shales, mudstones, shaly limestones, and coal seams.
The favourability map reveals a continuous, relatively diffuse shape with values exceeding 0.9.
The total area of this target is 371.48 km2.



7.4.2.5.2 Characterization of the target

7.4.2.5.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a continuous shape between compact and diffuse filling more
than 60% of the target (Figure 175). Values over 0.9 correlate with the known Pb-Zn
occurrences.



7.4.2.5.2.2 History

Mining in the area had started at least by 2014, with the main uptake in mineral extraction
around 2021. At least 3 main sites are being exploited on a considerable scale, however no
evidence of machinery or industrial processes is seen on satellite imagery. Several other
mineral occurrences and sites are either inactive or abandoned.



7.4.2.5.2.3 Geological Setting

The regional rocks are identified as Late Cretaceous and syn-rift as Awgu/Pindiga Formations,
composed of carbonaceous shales, mudstones, shaly limestones and coal seams (Cap1).

7.4.2.5.2.4 Mineralization

Middle Benue Trough. The combination of the DEM, potassium radiometric signal and the
magnetic signal suggest an intrusive body of great magnitude, suggesting a possible fluid
source for the mineralization and the existing mineral occurrences. Tilt derivative of the
magnetic signal indicates an anticline. Type of mineral genesis is interpreted as hydrothermal
and occurs as vein-like deposits. NIMEP exploration campaign has galena occurring within
siderite.

7.4.2.6 Target Pb-Zn 6

7.4.2.6.1 Summary

The target within the Upper Benue Trough comprises 5 occurrences of Pb-Zn, with one site
featuring recent small-scale mining operations, currently under the ownership of Geolank
Exploration Concepts Ltd, reporting an annual production of 741 metric tons of Pb-Zn. The
mineralized veins exhibit a predominant N-S trend, with the main body extending over 1.7
kilometres. These veins are hosted within late Cretaceous syn-rift sediments composed of
sandstones, limestones, and other carbonaceous rocks. The favourability map illustrates a
compact shape with values exceeding 0.9. The total area of this target is 792.12 km2.



7.4.2.6.2 Characterization of the target

7.4.2.6.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a continuous compact shape (sole for a hole in the middle) filling
more than 75% of the target (Figure 177). Values over 0.9 correlate with the known Pb-Zn
occurrences.



7.4.2.6.2.2 History

Mining activities can be traced back to 1985 possibly linked with British activities during the
colonial times. One main site showed considerable activity between 2012 and 2019, after
which no detectable changes to pit size or depth are identifiable. No evidence of machinery or
industrial processes is seen on satellite imagery, but a number of structures were built in the
area. Other mineral occurrences and sites are either inactive or abandoned.

            One main pit with length of at least 1.720m (Figure 173),

            All identified pits are either abandoned or inactive,

            The main orientation of the exploited veins is N-S.

            Reported Lead-Zinc mining production of 741t and 1750t for different sites.

7.4.2.6.2.3 Geological Setting

The regional rocks are identified as Late Cretaceous made up of syn-rift as Keana/Awe

limestones (Cka2), the Eze Aku/Yolde Formations, composed of carbonaceous shales,
limestones and fine to medium grained sandstone (Cey), and the Awgu/Pindinga Formations,
composed of carbonaceous shales, mudstones, shaly limestones and coal seams (Cap1,
Cap2 and Cap3).



7.4.2.6.2.4 Mineralization

At least 5 known occurrences of Lead-Zinc inside this target. The area lies on the northern
core of a double-plunging syncline and crosscut SSW-NNE by a double regional fault, possibly
generated during rifting. The Tilt derivative of the magnetic signal suggests that the limbs of
the fold are SE-NW trending. The trend of the veins is N-S with the type of mineral genesis
interpreted as hydrothermal. NIMEP exploration in the largest structure indicate a
steep/vertical dipping structure with grades up to 19.5 % Zn and 1.6% Pb.

7.4.2.7 Target Pb-Zn 7

7.4.2.7.1 Summary

The target within the Upper Benue Trough boasts 47 occurrences of Pb-Zn, with one site
currently undergoing recent industrial-scale mining operations and owned by Imperial JV
Limited, a joint venture company. Lead production in the target area has been reported to
reach 30 tons per annum by Aby Diamond Company. The mineralized veins exhibit a
predominant NNW-SSE trend, with the main body extending over 1.9 kilometres. These veins
are hosted within early and late Cretaceous syn-rift sediments comprised of sandstones and
siltstones. The favourability map depicts a compact shape with values exceeding 0.9. The total
area of this target is 365.37 km2.



7.4.2.7.2 Characterization of the target

7.4.2.7.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a continuous compact shape with some diffusing towards the
edges but filling more than 60% of the target (Figure 181: Favourability map for target Pb-Zn
8.Figure 181). Values over 0.9 correlate with most of the known Pb-Zn occurrences.



7.4.2.7.2.2 History

Mining activities can be traced back to at least 2003. One main site showed considerable
activity starting 2019 by showing a well-defined open-pit, along industrial heavy-duty
machinery allowing a constant extraction of ore. Big tailing mounds are spotted but no clear
evidence of a processing plant. Other mineral occurrences only show recent artisanal
workings, however satellite imagery only goes until 2020. The identified occurrences form a
41 km2 cluster area.



7.4.2.7.2.3 Geological Setting

The regional rocks on the rims of the anticline are identified as Late Cretaceous made up of
syn-rift as Keana/Awe Formations, composed of sandstones interbedded with siltstones

7.4.2.8 Target Pb-Zn 8

7.4.2.8.1 Summary

The target within the Lower Benue Trough features no reported occurrences of Pb-Zn but is
abundant in barite deposits. Mining operations within the area are entirely artisanal, with no
industrial equipment in use. Interestingly, the primary active mining zone currently lacks any
license claimant and remains open for exploitation. The mineralized veins predominantly trend
NE-SW, although the main mined body, approximately 350 meters in length, follows a
conjugate NW-SE direction. These occurrences are hosted within late Cretaceous syn-rift
sediments composed of sandstones and siltstones. The favourability map depicts a semi-
compact shape with values exceeding 0.9.



7.4.2.8.2 Characterization of the target

7.4.2.8.2.1 Favourability

The favourability map shows a continuous semi-compact shape filling more than 70% of the
target (Figure 181) and trending obliquely. Values over 0.9 correlate are common and correlate
with known barite mineral locations. The total area of this target is 542.28 km2.



7.4.2.8.2.2 History

Mining activities can be traced back to at least 2010 where several pits are visible. One site,
although known for several years before, only presented considerable activities starting 2021.
The lack of evidence of machinery, infrastructure or tailing accumulations suggest activities
remain artisanal.



7.4.2.8.2.3 Geological Setting

The regional rocks on the rims of the anticline are solely identified as Late Cretaceous made
up of syn-rift as Keana/Awe Formations, composed of sandstones interbedded with siltstones
(Cka1).

currently undergoing recent industrial-scale mining operations, owned by Imperial JV Limited,

depicts a compact shape with values exceeding 0.9. The total area of this target is 636.14 km2.

Figure 181: Favourability map for target Pb-Zn 8.(Figure 183). Values over 0.9 correlate with
the known Pb-Zn occurrences.

Mining activities can be traced back to at least 1985 possibly dating back to the colonial mining

The regional rocks are identified as Late Cretaceous made up of syn-rift as Keana/Awe
Formations, composed of sandstones interbedded with siltstones (Cka1).

7.4.2.9.2.4 Mineralization

At least 8 known occurrences of Lead-Zinc are reported inside this target. The area lies on the
Lower Benue Trough and includes at least 2 anticlines running NE-SW. The magnetic signal
in the area (1VD and Tilt) suggest that identifiable NW-SE trending features may control the
mineralization. The trend of the veins is NNW-SSE (conjugate to the regional faults?) with the
type of mineral genesis interpreted as hydrothermal.

            One main abandoned pit with length of at least 400m (Figure 161),

            All identified pits seem to be inactive/abandoned,

            The main orientation of the exploited veins is NW-SE,

            Reported Lead-Zinc mining production is unclear.



7.4.2.9.3 Prospect area:

The active area of 12,03km2 contains the following characteristics:

        Length of the main structure: 400m

        Average width of pits: 50m

        Number of occurrences: 2

        Status: active

7.5           Uranium (U) Targets

7.5.1 Identification and Location

7.5.1.1 Criteria Catalogue

Due to the scarcity of mineral occurrences, a Uranium MPM was not created. However, due
to its relevance and current market value (see chapter 3.4), an analysis of available data is
possible to select targets.

In Nigeria, the following genetic types of U-occurrences are possible:

        Granite related Uranium, either in hydrothermal veins in or around the intrusives or as
         disseminated U in the granites

        Sandstone hosted Uranium

        Uranium related to phosphorites

        Uranium in accessory minerals in various granitoid and alkaline rocks /(pyrochlore,
         monazite, xenotime, and similar minerals.

Generally, because of the presence of suitable geological environments, the discovery of big
deposits seems to be possible. At this moment, no ranking is possible as the knowledge about
the mineral potential of the different genetic types of U occurrences is scarce. The following
criteria for selection of areas was used:

        eU/eTh values high:

                                eU/eTh values high
                                More than > 1.0
                                Between 1.0 and 0.6
                                Between 0.6 and 0.4
                                Between 0.4 and 0.2
                                Less than 0.2

        eUranium values high:

                                            eUranium content high             Score
                                                    Yes                         5
                                                    No                          1

7.5.2 Description of targets

7.5.2.1 Target Uranium 1

Location: Lower Benue Trough, Benue State.

Geology: Sandstones, shales, mudstones and coal (Cnl and Cms – Late Cretaceous post-rift).

Mining activities: At least three important coal deposits are being mined along the same
sedimentary layers further to the East of the identified area (Figure 185).

Additional information: high values of eUranium are detected in the area and correlated with
points of high value of the ratio of eU/eTh (Figure 186). It is recommend to investigate the ash
of the coal for its U content, and searching for U in the sandstones accompanying the coal
seams.

Total area: 769.18 km2.

                        (7°25’33”N 7°46’35”E). Image taken from Google Earth.

7.5.2.2 Target Uranium 2

Location: Eastern Basement Complex and Upper Benue Trough, Taraba State.

Geology: Mainly sandstones (Cbs1 and Cbs2 – Early Cretaceous syn-rift) delimited to the north
by a main fault structure and separating younger sediments (Cka1 – Late Cretaceous syn-rift).
A main syn-tectonic granite (Nog – Neoproterozoic to Cambrian) is located south of the target
area.

Mining activities: No mining activity is seen in the area, however two known occurrences have
been identified in the area:

        Mayo Lope prospect (northern area of the target): with detected concentrations of
         0.18% and 0.25% U in sandstones (Olade M. , 2020).

        Mika prospect (southern area of the target): veins and disseminations of Uranium in a
         brecciated granite with grades of 0.63% U (Karniliyus & Akedegwa, 2018).

Additional information: high values of eUranium are linked to the intruding granite that has
probably been weathered, with fluids carrying the Uranium downslope and depositing in the
sandstones as an infiltration mechanism.

Total area: 2,601.98 km2 (Figure 187).

7.5.2.3 Target Uranium 3

Location: south-western corner of Nigeria, Ogun State.

Geology: Late cretaceous sandstones, shales, mudstones and coal.

Mining activities: Several mine sites exist in the area for commodities unrelated to Uranium.

Additional information: high values of eUranium are detected in the area and correlated with
ratios of eU/eTh (Figure 188). The shape is very compact indicating that the concentrations of
Uranium are extensive and follow the bedding regionally and discarding the possibility of river
accumulation.

Total area: 577.09 km2.

7.5.2.4 Target Uranium 4

Location: north-western corner of Nigeria, Sokoto State.

Geology: Paleogene to Neogene laterites sandstones and clays (Tgw2) on top of late
cretaceous undifferentiated sandstone, shale, siltstone and claystone (Cwt2).

Mining activities: Phosphate mine sites are documented in the area (Figure 189). The known
U grade in the phosphorites reach up to 29-65 ppm (Ogunleye P. O., 2002) (Mustapha, 2022)
.

Additional information: As U may enrich in lateritic formations as well, it is recommended to
verify serval points with pits and measure the U grades in situ using pXRF devices. The total
area interpreted to hold this mineralization is estimated to have a total size of 8,196 km2.

Total area: 1,458.93 km2.

7.5.2.5 Target Uranium 5

Location: Upper Benue Trough, Borno State.

Geology: Migmatitic gneisses with some interpreted volcanic dykes trending SW-NE.

Mining activities: No known mining area or activity has been registered within the target area.
At least three locations of Uranium occurrences have been reported in the area and seen to
correlate very well with a linear magnetic anomaly suggesting a tectonically controlled (vein
type?) deposit (Figure 191Fehler! Verweisquelle konnte nicht gefunden werden.):

        Gubrunde: no documented information on U concentrations.

        Kanawa: average U concentrations are 0.1wt. % (Bute, Yang, Yang, Kayode, & Lu,
         2022)

        Zona Propsect: average concentrations are around 650 ppm.

Additional information: high values of eUranium in a very compact shape are detected in the
area. However, very high values of eTh are also detected.

Total area: 657.40 km2 (Figure 191).

7.5.2.6 Target Uranium 6

Location: around Kisi town, Oyo State.

Geology: according to the 2M geology, the rocks in the area are banded gneisses along with
late syn-tectonic Older Granites. However, the radiometric response indicates mainly an
intrusive body with a very elongate shape.

Mining activities: No known mining area or activity has been registered within the target area.

Additional information: the target contains the highest eUranium values of Nigeria in a very
well defined shape. However, very high values of eTh are also detected. Nonetheless,
nowhere else in Nigeria such structure exists. The Potassium radiometric signal suggests an
intrusive body that has disseminated Uranium, and the RTP 1VD signal indicates a prominent
fault cross-cutting the body. Studies on accessory minerals in granites have indicated that
uraniferous pyrochlore can occur with concentrations of up to 3.5 wt. % UO2 and up to 4.3 wt.
% ThO2 (Beer, 1952).

Total area: 538.56 km2 (Figure 192).

7.5.2.7 Target Uranium 7

Location: Lower Benue Trough, Benue State.

Geology: Sandstones, shales, mudstones and coal.

Mining activities: No documented occurrences or mining activities are documented in the area.

Additional information: high values of eUranium are detected in the area and correlated with
points of high value of the ratio of eU/eTh (Figure 193). The values follow the geology mapped
in the area suggesting an accumulation of uranium as infiltration and not as lateritic enrichment.
Intrusives in the basement complex with high eU values suggest a possible U source for the
formation of sandstone hosted Uranium. Documented coal occurrences also indicated
favourability for Uranium in the area (please check 7.5.2.1)

Total area: 1,342.89 km2.

8.             Recommendations

8.1            Further Strategies

National and regional activities:

         Further develop predictive maps with the following considerations:

              o     Integrate the new geophysical airborne data,

              o     Incorporate the new geochemical data,

              o     Add mine production as available.

         Release of new maps (through the DSS) through press releases (plus similar marketing
          strategies).

Thematic Commodity Activities:

         Clarify the prospectivity by reconnaissance studies, while identifying the mineralization
          type (sandstones, phosphorites?)

         Determine further the prospectivity of pegmatites with reconnaissance studies (identify
          industrial pegmatites)

         Understand the metallogeny of the alkaline complexes directed to Niobium, Tantalum,
          Lithium, Rare-earth elements and, possibly, carbonatites.

         Clarify further potential commodities (Cu, Ni, Cr.



Local activities

         Block prospective tenements free of licenses (focus on areas with known mineral
          occurrences),

         Focus on relevant/future metals: Au, U, Li, Nb, Ta and Sn,

         geochemical campaigns on identified prospective areas (targets) for targeted
          exploration and identify key objectives,

         Execute a drilling campaign on the detected objectives.




Status    draft                     Version 1.0        Date created:   Date changed:   Date printed:        Page 334
          release               x                      13/05/2024      24/05/2024
              MINERAL SECTOR SUPPORT FOR ECONOMIC DIVERSIFICATION (MINDIVER)
              Mining Investment Facilitation Project
              D6 Project Final Report                                                                  Project 20220074




8.2            Further Improvements

To continue improving the existing system, the following activities shall be made critical in
subsequent implementations:

         Direct link to the mining cadastral data to keep the information up-to-date in the DSS,

         Direct link to the Geodata Centre information stored in the NGSA premises,

         Implement a data release functionality for data to be monetized (e.g., sales portal),

         Classify the existing data from the Ministry and NGSA into confidential and public (free
          or on sale) for organization and transparency,

         Perform data improvement activities by the back-end users (the agencies):

              o     Addition of new data (e.g., exploration reports),

              o     Improve data (e.g. mineral occurrence data, new geological maps)

              o     Integrate the NMRDSS backend into the business processes at NGSA, ASM,
                    IPMT, etc.

         Analyze the business processes and identify the interaction with the system (e.g.,
          annual reporting about buying centers),

         Integrate with other relevant ministries and keep track of relevant updates from their
          data (e.g., national planning, infrastructure, agriculture)

         Execute extensive training activities for the following aspects:

              o     IT services: hardware and IT-infrastructure, database and GIS web services.

              o     Geological aspects: economic geology, predictive mapping, commodities.

              o     Mining aspects: locations, activities, production, operators.

         Data
                   Discussion with PGW ongoing on ground-truthing data to be provided from
                    analyses from Airborne Geophysical Survey. PGW has shared their
                    groundtruthing program for selected sites scheduled for the 2nd week of March.
                   The first batch of the geochemical mapping data has been shared for use by the
                    MIF team.
                   Data on selected areas and commodities are still pending from the MI
                    department.

         Mining Conferences
                   Preparation for attendance and presentation at the PDAC conference (Canada)
                    happening in March.

         Stakeholder Engagements and Investment Facilitation
                   There are ongoing engagements with mining exploration companies to
                    understand their current state, technology in place for mining in the country.
                   Engagements with MDAs are ongoing for data required by the technical team to
                    continue their study, however, responses from the MDAs have been slow,
                    affecting the timely completion of deliverables.
                   There is scheduled engagement with IPMT and PIU to understand the
                    investment facilitation context for Nigeria and investors’ expectations. The goal
                    of this is plan a fit-for-purpose training curriculum on investment facilitation.
